````math
$`1-\delta`$, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{t+1})=\mathbb{E}_{q\sim p_{0}}\mathbb{E}_{a\sim\pi_{\hat{\theta}_{t+1}}(\cdot\mid q)}[s(q,a)]\;\geq\;\tau\big(1-\bar{\delta}_{t}^{(p_{0})}\big)\;\geq\;\tau\left(1-\frac{Z^{(m)}_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{t})}{\alpha^{(m)}(\hat{\theta}_{t})}\,\sqrt{\frac{2\log\!\big(|\Pi|\,\delta^{-1}\big)}{n_{t}^{\smash{(m)}}}}\right).
``` |  |

Recall the notation $`\alpha(\hat{\theta}_{t},q)\in[0,1]`$ and
$`\alpha(\hat{\theta}_{t})\coloneqq\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{q}\alpha(\hat{\theta}_{t},q)>0`$.
For $`m\in\mathbb{N}`$ define

|     |                                             |     |
|-----|---------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       f_{m}(x)\ :=\ 1-(1-x)^{m},\qquad x\in[0,1].
       ```                                          |     |

Then

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\alpha^{(m)}(\hat{\theta}_{t},q)=f_{m}(\alpha(\hat{\theta}_{t},q)),\quad\alpha^{(m)}(\hat{\theta}_{t})=\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{q}f_{m}\big(\alpha(\hat{\theta}_{t},q)\big)=f_{m}(\alpha(\hat{\theta}_{t})),\quad Z^{(m)}_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{t})=\mathbb{E}_{q\sim p_{0}}\!\big[f_{m}(\alpha(\hat{\theta}_{t},q))\big].
``` |  |

Define, for $`y\in[0,1)`$,

|     |                                          |     |
|-----|------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       h_{m}(y)\ :=\ \frac{1-y^{m+1}}{1-y^{m}}.
       ```                                       |     |

Using $`f_{m+1}(x)/f_{m}(x)=h_{m}(1-x)`$, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{Z^{(m+1)}_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{t})}{\alpha^{(m+1)}(\hat{\theta}_{t})}=\mathbb{E}\!\left[\frac{f_{m+1}(\alpha(\hat{\theta}_{t},q))}{f_{m}(\alpha(\hat{\theta}_{t},q))}\cdot\frac{f_{m}(\alpha(\hat{\theta}_{t},q))}{f_{m}(\alpha(\hat{\theta}_{t}))}\cdot\frac{f_{m}(\alpha(\hat{\theta}_{t}))}{f_{m+1}(\alpha(\hat{\theta}_{t}))}\right]=\mathbb{E}_{q\sim p_{0}}\!\left[\frac{h_{m}(1-\alpha(\hat{\theta}_{t},q))}{h_{m}(1-\alpha(\hat{\theta}_{t}))}\cdot\frac{f_{m}(\alpha(\hat{\theta}_{t},q))}{f_{m}(\alpha(\hat{\theta}_{t}))}\right].
``` |  |

We claim $`h_{m}`$ is increasing on $`[0,1)`$. Indeed,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}y}\log h_{m}(y)=\frac{y^{m-1}(m-(m+1)y+y^{m+1})}{(1-y^{m})(1-y^{m+1})}\ \geq\ 0,
``` |  |

since the $`m-(m+1)y+y^{m+1}`$ is decreasing in $`y`$ with value $`m`$
at $`y=0`$ and $`0`$ at $`y=1`$. Because
$`\alpha(\hat{\theta}_{t},q)\geq\alpha(\hat{\theta}_{t})`$ a.s., we have
$`1-\alpha(\hat{\theta}_{t},q)\leq 1-\alpha(\hat{\theta}_{t})`$ and
hence
$`h_{m}(1-\alpha(\hat{\theta}_{t},q))\leq h_{m}(1-\alpha(\hat{\theta}_{t}))`$.
Therefore,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{Z^{(m+1)}_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{t})}{\alpha^{(m+1)}(\hat{\theta}_{t})}\ \leq\ \frac{Z^{(m)}_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{t})}{\alpha^{(m)}(\hat{\theta}_{t})}.
``` |  |

Since $`1>\alpha(\hat{\theta}_{t})>0`$, we have
$`f_{m}(\alpha(\hat{\theta}_{t},q))\uparrow 1`$ for every $`q`$ and
$`f_{m}(\alpha(\hat{\theta}_{t}))\uparrow 1`$ as $`m\to\infty`$. By the
monotone convergence theorem,
$`Z^{(m)}_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{t})=\mathbb{E}[f_{m}(\alpha(\hat{\theta}_{t},q))]\to 1`$
and
$`\alpha^{(m)}(\hat{\theta}_{t})=f_{m}(\alpha(\hat{\theta}_{t}))\to 1`$,
hence

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\lim_{m\to\infty}\frac{Z^{(m)}_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{t})}{\alpha^{(m)}(\hat{\theta}_{t})}=1.
``` |  |

∎

###### Lemma B.1 ([Wong and Shen \[35\]](#bib.bib32), [Geer \[11\]](#bib.bib21), [Zhang \[46\]](#bib.bib33), [Huang et al. \[17\]](#bib.bib9)).

Fix iteration $`t`$ and $`m\geq 1`$. Let $`\mathcal{Q}`$ be the question
space and $`\Delta(\mathcal{A})`$ the set of probability measures on the
answer space $`\mathcal{A}`$. Let
$`\Pi\subset(\mathcal{Q}\to\Delta(\mathcal{A}))`$ be a finite model
class and suppose the population optimizer
$`\pi_{\theta^{\star}_{t+1}}(\cdot\mid q)=p^{\prime}_{\hat{\theta}_{t}}(\cdot\mid q)`$
belongs to $`\Pi`$. Draw $`n^{(m)}_{t}`$ accepted samples i.i.d.

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
(q_{i},a_{i})\ \sim\ D^{\prime(m)}_{p_{0},\hat{\theta}_{t}}(q,a)\;=\;p^{\prime(m)}_{p_{0},\hat{\theta}_{t}}(q)\,\pi_{\theta^{\star}_{t+1}}(a\mid q),\qquad i=1,\dots,n^{(m)}_{t},
``` |  |

and define the empirical MLE

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\hat{\theta}_{t+1}\ \in\ \arg\max_{\theta:\,\pi_{\theta}\in\Pi}\;\sum_{i=1}^{n^{(m)}_{t}}\log\pi_{\theta}(a_{i}\mid q_{i}).
``` |  |

Then for any $`\delta\in(0,1)`$, with probability at least $`1-\delta`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}_{q\sim p^{\prime(m)}_{p_{0},\hat{\theta}_{t}}}\!\Big[D_{\mathrm{H}}^{2}\big(\pi_{\hat{\theta}_{t+1}}(\cdot\mid q),\,\pi_{\theta^{\star}_{t+1}}(\cdot\mid q)\big)\Big]\;\leq\;\frac{2\log\!\big(|\Pi|\,\delta^{-1}\big)}{n^{(m)}_{t}}.
``` |  |

#### B.2  Proof of Corollary [4.4](#S4.Thmtheorem4 "Corollary 4.4. ‣ 4.2 Multi-Step Self-Improvement ‣ 4 Iterative Self-Improvement ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula")

###### Proof.

Fix iteration $`t`$, recall

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
Z_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{t})=\mathbb{E}_{q\sim p_{0}}\!\big[\alpha(\hat{\theta}_{t},q)\big],\qquad\alpha(\hat{\theta}_{t},q)=\Pr_{a\sim\pi_{\hat{\theta}_{t}}(\cdot\mid q)}\!\big[s(q,a)\geq\tau\,\big|\,q\big].
``` |  |

When $`s\in\{0,1\}`$ and $`\tau\in(0,1]`$, we have
$`\mathbf{1}_{\{s(q,a)\geq\tau\}}=s(q,a)`$, hence
$`\alpha(\hat{\theta}_{t},q)=\mathbb{E}_{a\sim\pi_{\hat{\theta}_{t}}(\cdot\mid q)}\!\big[s(q,a)\big].`$
Averaging over $`q\sim p_{0}`$ yields

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
Z_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{t})=\mathbb{E}_{q\sim p_{0}}\mathbb{E}_{a\sim\pi_{\hat{\theta}_{t}}(\cdot\mid q)}\!\big[s(q,a)\big]=V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{t}).
``` |  |

Moreover, since $`s\in\{0,1\}`$, for any fixed $`q`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}_{a\sim\pi_{\hat{\theta}_{t+1}}(\cdot\mid q)}[s(q,a)]=\Pr_{a\sim\pi_{\hat{\theta}_{t+1}}(\cdot\mid q)}[s(q,a)=1]=1-\delta_{t}(q),
``` |  |

where
$`\delta_{t}(q):=\Pr_{a\sim\pi_{\hat{\theta}_{t+1}}(\cdot\mid q)}[s(q,a)<\tau].`$
Therefore,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{t+1})=\mathbb{E}_{q\sim p_{0}}\mathbb{E}_{a\sim\pi_{\hat{\theta}_{t+1}}(\cdot\mid q)}[s(q,a)]=1-\bar{\delta}_{t}^{(p_{0})},\qquad\bar{\delta}_{t}^{(p_{0})}:=\mathbb{E}_{q\sim p_{0}}[\delta_{t}(q)].
``` |  |

At iteration $`t`$, we propose $`n`$ i.i.d. pairs
$`(q_{i},a_{i})\sim p_{0}(q)\,\pi_{\hat{\theta}_{t}}(a\mid q)`$, and
accept those with $`s(q_{i},a_{i})\geq\tau`$. Let
$`X_{i}:=\mathbf{1}_{\{s(q_{i},a_{i})\geq\tau\}}\in\{0,1\}`$ and
$`n_{t}:=\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}`$. Then, by the binary reward identity
above,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]=\mathbb{E}_{q\sim p_{0}}\Pr_{a\sim\pi_{\hat{\theta}_{t}}(\cdot\mid q)}[s(q,a)\geq\tau\mid q]=Z_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{t})=V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{t}).
``` |  |

Hoeffding’s inequality gives, for any $`\epsilon>0`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Pr\!\left[\frac{n_{t}}{n}\leq V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{t})-\epsilon\right]\leq\exp(-2n\epsilon^{2}).
``` |  |

Choosing $`\epsilon=\sqrt{\log(\delta^{\prime-1})/(2n)}`$ implies that
with probability at least $`1-\delta^{\prime}`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{n_{t}}{n}\ \geq\ V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{t})-\sqrt{\frac{\log(\delta^{\prime-1})}{2n}}.
``` |  |

Define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{G}_{t}:=\Big\{q:\ \alpha(\hat{\theta}_{t},q)\ \geq\ c\,V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{t})\Big\}.
``` |  |

By
Assumption [4.3](#S4.Thmtheorem3 "Assumption 4.3. ‣ 4.2 Multi-Step Self-Improvement ‣ 4 Iterative Self-Improvement ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula"),
$`\Pr_{q\sim p_{0}}\!\big[q\notin\mathcal{G}_{t}\big]\ \leq\ \gamma.`$
Since $`\delta_{t}(q)\in[0,1]`$, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\bar{\delta}_{t}^{(p_{0})}=\mathbb{E}_{q\sim p_{0}}\!\big[\delta_{t}(q)\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{G}_{t}}\big]+\mathbb{E}_{q\sim p_{0}}\!\big[\delta_{t}(q)\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{G}_{t}^{\mathrm{c}}}\big]\ \leq\ \mathbb{E}_{q\sim p_{0}}\!\big[\delta_{t}(q)\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{G}_{t}}\big]+\Pr_{q\sim p_{0}}\!\big[q\notin\mathcal{G}_{t}\big]\ \leq\ \mathbb{E}_{q\sim p_{0}}\!\big[\delta_{t}(q)\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{G}_{t}}\big]+\gamma.
``` |  |

Furthermore,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}_{q\sim p_{0}}\!\big[\delta_{t}(q)\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{G}_{t}}\big]=\mathbb{E}_{q\sim p^{\prime}_{p_{0},\hat{\theta}_{t}}}\!\left[\frac{Z_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{t})}{\alpha(\hat{\theta}_{t},q)}\,\delta_{t}(q)\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{G}_{t}}\right]\ \leq\ \frac{1}{c}\,\mathbb{E}_{q\sim p^{\prime}_{p_{0},\hat{\theta}_{t}}}\!\big[\delta_{t}(q)\big],
``` |  |

where we use the fact that On $`\mathcal{G}_{t}`$, we have
$`\alpha(\hat{\theta}_{t},q)\geq cV_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{t})=cZ_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{t})`$.
Thus

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\bar{\delta}_{t}^{(p_{0})}\ \leq\ \gamma+\frac{1}{c}\,\mathbb{E}_{q\sim p^{\prime}_{p_{0},\hat{\theta}_{t}}}\!\big[\delta_{t}(q)\big].
``` |  |

By repeating the identical argument in the proof of
Theorem [4.1](#S4.Thmtheorem1 "Theorem 4.1. ‣ 4.1 Single-Step Self-Improvement ‣ 4 Iterative Self-Improvement ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula")
with $`m=1`$, we obtain that with probability at least $`1-\delta`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}_{q\sim p^{\prime}_{p_{0},\hat{\theta}_{t}}}\!\big[\delta_{t}(q)\big]\ \leq\ \sqrt{\frac{2\log\!\big(|\Pi|\,\delta^{-1}\big)}{n_{t}}}.
``` |  |

Then, we get (w.p. $`\geq 1-\delta`$):

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{t+1})=1-\bar{\delta}_{t}^{(p_{0})}\ \geq\ 1-\gamma-\frac{1}{c}\sqrt{\frac{2\log\!\big(|\Pi|\,\delta^{-1}\big)}{n_{t}}}.
``` |  |

Moreover, w.p. $`\geq 1-\delta^{\prime}`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{t}}}\ \leq\ \frac{1}{\sqrt{\,n\Big(V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{t})-\sqrt{\log(\delta^{\prime-1})/(2n)}\Big)\,}}.
``` |  |

Therefore, by a union bound over the two events, with probability at
least $`1-\delta-\delta^{\prime}`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{t+1})\;\geq\;1-\gamma-\frac{1}{c}\,\sqrt{\frac{2\log\!\big(|\Pi|\delta^{-1}\big)/n}{\,V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{t})\;-\;\sqrt{\log(\delta^{\prime-1})/(2n)}}}\,.
``` |  |

Define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
F(x)\;:=\;1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{x-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}},\qquad\nu:=\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}},\qquad c_{\delta}:=\sqrt{2\log\!\big(|\Pi|\,\delta^{-1}\big)},\qquad c_{\delta^{\prime}}:=\sqrt{\frac{\log(\delta^{\prime-1})}{2}},
``` |  |

on its natural domain $`x>c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu`$.Then the preceding
bound can be rewritten as

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{t+1})\;\geq\;F\big(V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{t})\big).
``` |  |

Finally, since $`F`$ is monotone increasing on its domain, iterating the
one-step inequality yields that, with probability at least
$`1-t(\delta+\delta^{\prime})`$, for every integer $`t\geq 0`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{t})\;\geq\;F^{\circ t}\big(V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})\big),
``` |  |

where $`F^{\circ t}`$ denotes the $`t`$-fold composition of $`F`$. This
completes the proof.

∎

#### B.3  Proof of Proposition [4.5](#S4.Thmtheorem5 "Proposition 4.5. ‣ 4.2 Multi-Step Self-Improvement ‣ 4 Iterative Self-Improvement ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula")

###### Proof.

This proposition is an immediate specialization of
Corollary [B.3](#A2.Thmtheorem3 "Corollary B.3. ‣ B.3 Proof of Proposition ‣ B. Proofs for Section ‣ Appendix: Proofs ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula")
by taking $`a=1`$. ∎

###### Lemma B.2.

Let

|     |                                                      |     |
|-----|------------------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       F(x)\;=\;1-\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{x}},\qquad x\in(0,1),
       ```                                                   |     |

with parameter $`\sigma>0`$. Assume $`0<\sigma<\sqrt{4/27}.`$ Let
$`x_{-}<x_{+}`$ denote the two solutions in $`(0,1)`$ of
$`x(1-x)^{2}=\sigma^{2}.`$ Then,

|     |                                                |     |
|-----|------------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       x_{+}-x_{-}\geq 1-\frac{3\sqrt{3}}{2}\,\sigma,
       ```                                             |     |

and for every $`x\in(x_{-},x_{+})`$ and every integer $`t\geq 0`$, all
iterates $`F^{\circ t}(x)`$ stay in $`(x_{-},x_{+})`$ and satisfy
$`F^{\circ(t+1)}(x)\;>\;F^{\circ t}(x).`$

###### Proof.

Consider

|     |                                        |     |
|-----|----------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       h(x)\;:=\;x(1-x)^{2},\qquad x\in[0,1].
       ```                                     |     |

Then $`h^{\prime}(x)=(1-x)^{2}-2x(1-x)=(1-x)(1-3x),`$ so $`h`$ is
strictly increasing on $`(0,1/3)`$ and strictly decreasing on
$`(1/3,1)`$, with a unique interior maximizer at $`x=1/3`$. Moreover,
$`h(1/3)=4/27.`$ So whenever $`\sigma^{2}<4/27`$, the equation
$`h(x)=\sigma^{2}`$ has exactly two distinct solutions in $`(0,1)`$; we
denote them by $`x_{-}<x_{+}`$.

Moreover, expanding $`h(x)=\sigma^{2}`$ gives the cubic
$`x^{3}-2x^{2}+x-\sigma^{2}=0.`$ Set $`x=z+2/3,`$ we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
x^{3}-2x^{2}+x-\sigma^{2}=z^{3}-\frac{1}{3}z+\Big(\frac{2}{27}-\sigma^{2}\Big)=0.
``` |  |

Trigonometric solution of the roots of this depressed cubic is

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
z_{\ell}=\frac{2}{3}\cos\Big[\frac{1}{3}\arccos\!\Big(-1+\frac{27}{2}\sigma^{2}\Big)-\frac{2\pi\ell}{3}\Big],\qquad\ell=0,1,2.
``` |  |

so

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
x_{\ell}=\frac{2}{3}+\frac{2}{3}\cos\Big[\frac{1}{3}\arccos\!\Big(-1+\frac{27}{2}\sigma^{2}\Big)-\frac{2\pi\ell}{3}\Big],\qquad\ell=0,1,2.
``` |  |

Define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
u\;:=\;\frac{1}{3}\arccos\!\Big(-1+\frac{27}{2}\sigma^{2}\Big)\in\Big(0,\frac{\pi}{3}\Big),
``` |  |

then

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
x_{+}=\frac{2}{3}+\frac{2}{3}\cos\Big(u-\frac{2\pi}{3}\Big),\qquad x_{-}=\frac{2}{3}+\frac{2}{3}\cos\Big(u-\frac{4\pi}{3}\Big).
``` |  |

Hence

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
x_{+}-x_{-}=\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}\sin u=\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}\,\sin\!\left(\frac{1}{3}\arccos\!\Big(-1+\frac{27}{2}\sigma^{2}\Big)\right).
``` |  |

We now prove the desired bound
$`x_{+}-x_{-}\;\geq\;1-\frac{3\sqrt{3}}{2}\,\sigma.`$ Let
$`\cos\theta:=(3\sqrt{3}\sigma)/2\in(0,1)`$ with $`\theta\in[0,\pi/2]`$,
then

|     |                                                            |     |
|-----|------------------------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       -1+\frac{27}{2}\sigma^{2}=2\cos^{2}\theta-1=\cos(2\theta),
       ```                                                         |     |

Hence $`x_{+}-x_{-}=\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}\sin\Big(\frac{2\theta}{3}\Big).`$
and $`1-\frac{3\sqrt{3}}{2}\,\sigma=1-\cos\theta.`$

Thus it suffices to show that for all $`\theta\in[0,\pi/2]`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}\sin\Big(\frac{2\theta}{3}\Big)\;\geq\;1-\cos\theta.
``` |  |

Define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
g(\theta):=\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}\sin\Big(\frac{2\theta}{3}\Big)-\big(1-\cos\theta\big),\qquad\theta\in[0,\frac{\pi}{2}].
``` |  |

We have $`g(0)=g(\pi/2)=0.`$ Moreover,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
g^{\prime\prime}(\theta)=-\frac{8}{9\sqrt{3}}\sin\Big(\frac{2\theta}{3}\Big)-\cos\theta\leq 0\
``` |  |

for $`\theta\in[0,\pi/2]`$ since we have $`\sin(2\theta/3)\geq 0`$ and
$`\cos\theta\geq 0`$, thus $`g`$ is concave on $`[0,\pi/2]`$ and
vanishes at both endpoints. So $`g(\theta)\geq 0,`$ which is equivalent
to

|     |                                                   |     |
|-----|---------------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       x_{+}-x_{-}\;\geq\;1-\frac{3\sqrt{3}}{2}\,\sigma.
       ```                                                |     |

Next, we characterize where $`F(x)>x`$. For $`x\in(0,1)`$, this is
equivalent to $`\sigma^{2}<x(1-x)^{2}.`$ Recall that $`x_{\pm}`$ are the
two solutions to $`x(1-x)^{2}=\sigma^{2}`$ in $`(0,1)`$, and that
$`h(x)=x(1-x)^{2}`$ is strictly increasing on $`(0,1/3)`$ and strictly
decreasing on $`(1/3,1)`$. Hence $`x(1-x)^{2}>\sigma^{2}`$ is equivalent
to $`x\in(x_{-},x_{+})`$, so we have $`F(x)>x`$ is equivalent to
$`x\in(x_{-},x_{+})`$. Let $`x\in(x_{-},x_{+})`$ and define
$`x_{t}:=F^{\circ t}(x)`$ for $`t\geq 0`$.

We prove by induction that

|     |                                                                |     |
|-----|----------------------------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       x_{-}<x_{t}<x_{+},\qquad x_{t+1}>x_{t},\qquad\forall\,t\geq 0.
       ```                                                             |     |

For $`t=0`$, we have $`x_{0}=x\in(x_{-},x_{+})`$ by assumption, and
$`x_{1}=F(x_{0})>x_{0}`$. Using monotonicity of $`F`$ and
$`F(x_{\pm})=x_{\pm}`$,

|     |                                               |     |
|-----|-----------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       x_{-}=F(x_{-})<F(x_{0})=x_{1}<F(x_{+})=x_{+},
       ```                                            |     |

so $`x_{1}\in(x_{-},x_{+})`$. Assume now that $`x_{t}\in(x_{-},x_{+})`$
and $`x_{t}>x_{t-1}`$ for some $`t\geq 1`$. Then, since
$`x_{t}\in(x_{-},x_{+})`$, $`x_{t+1}=F(x_{t})>x_{t}.`$ Furthermore,
monotonicity of $`F`$ and the fixed-point property at $`x_{\pm}`$ give

|     |                                                 |     |
|-----|-------------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       x_{-}=F(x_{-})<F(x_{t})=x_{t+1}<F(x_{+})=x_{+},
       ```                                              |     |

so $`x_{t+1}\in(x_{-},x_{+})`$. This completes the induction and shows
that for every integer $`t\geq 0`$,
$`F^{\circ(t+1)}(x)\;>\;F^{\circ t}(x).`$ ∎

###### Corollary B.3.

Let

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
F(x;a,\nu)\;:=\;1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{ax-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}},\qquad x>\frac{c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}{a}.
``` |  |

Assume $`(a,\nu)`$ satisfies the validity conditions in
Definition [A.1](#A1.Thmtheorem1 "Definition A.1 (ℐ(𝑎,𝜈)). ‣ A.2 Deferred Definitions and Explicit Expressions ‣ A. Notation and Deferred Definitions ‣ Appendix: Proofs ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula"),
so that the interval

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{I}(a,\nu)\;:=\;\big(x_{-}(a,\nu),\,x_{+}(a,\nu)\big)\ \subset\ \left(\frac{c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}{a},\,1-\gamma\right)
``` |  |

in
Definition [A.1](#A1.Thmtheorem1 "Definition A.1 (ℐ(𝑎,𝜈)). ‣ A.2 Deferred Definitions and Explicit Expressions ‣ A. Notation and Deferred Definitions ‣ Appendix: Proofs ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula")
is well-defined. Then, for any $`x\in\mathcal{I}(a,\nu)`$ and any
integer $`t\geq 0`$, $`F^{\circ t}(x;a,\nu)\in\mathcal{I}(a,\nu)`$ and
$`F^{\circ(t+1)}(x;a,\nu)>F^{\circ t}(x;a,\nu)`$. Moreover, the interval
length satisfies

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
|\mathcal{I}(a,\nu)|\;\geq\;\left(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}{a}\right)-\frac{3\sqrt{3}}{2}\cdot\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{\,a(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\,}}.
``` |  |

Furthermore, the family $`\{\mathcal{I}(a,\nu)\}`$ is monotone in the
sense of inclusion: (i) for fixed $`\nu`$, if $`a_{2}>a_{1}>0`$, then
$`\mathcal{I}(a_{1},\nu)\subset\mathcal{I}(a_{2},\nu)`$; (ii) for fixed
$`a`$, if $`\nu_{2}>\nu_{1}`$, then
$`\mathcal{I}(a,\nu_{2})\subset\mathcal{I}(a,\nu_{1})`$.

###### Proof.

We follow the notations in
Definition [A.1](#A1.Thmtheorem1 "Definition A.1 (ℐ(𝑎,𝜈)). ‣ A.2 Deferred Definitions and Explicit Expressions ‣ A. Notation and Deferred Definitions ‣ Appendix: Proofs ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula").
Define the affine change of variables

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
x=\frac{c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}{a}+\left(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}{a}\right)\,y,\qquad y\in(0,1),
``` |  |

A direct substitution shows that for every $`y\in(0,1)`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle F\Big(\frac{c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}{a}+\big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}{a}\big)\,y;a,\nu\Big)`$ | $`\displaystyle=1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a\big(\frac{c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}{a}+(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}{a})y\big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{\big(a(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\big)\,y}}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\frac{c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}{a}+\left(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}{a}\right)\left(1-\frac{a\,c_{\delta}\nu}{c\,(a(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{y}}\right).`$ |  |

Therefore, if we denote

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
g_{a,\nu}(y):=1-\frac{a\,c_{\delta}\nu}{c\,(a(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{y}},\qquad y\in(0,1),
``` |  |

then we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
F\Big(\frac{c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}{a}+\big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}{a}\big)\,y;a,\nu\Big)=\frac{c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}{a}+\left(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}{a}\right)\,g_{a,\nu}(y).
``` |  |

The map $`g_{a,\nu}`$ is exactly of the form in
Lemma [B.2](#A2.Thmtheorem2 "Lemma B.2. ‣ B.3 Proof of Proposition ‣ B. Proofs for Section ‣ Appendix: Proofs ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula")
with parameter

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{a\,c_{\delta}\nu}{c\,(a(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}\in\Big(0,\sqrt{\frac{4}{27}}\Big).
``` |  |

Then, applying
Lemma [B.2](#A2.Thmtheorem2 "Lemma B.2. ‣ B.3 Proof of Proposition ‣ B. Proofs for Section ‣ Appendix: Proofs ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula")
to $`g_{a,\nu}`$ yields: for every
$`y\in\big(y_{-}(a,\nu),y_{+}(a,\nu)\big)`$ and every $`t\geq 0`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
g_{a,\nu}^{\circ t}(y)\in\big(y_{-}(a,\nu),y_{+}(a,\nu)\big)\quad\text{and}\quad g_{a,\nu}^{\circ(t+1)}(y)>g_{a,\nu}^{\circ t}(y),
``` |  |

and moreover

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
y_{+}(a,\nu)-y_{-}(a,\nu)\;\geq\;1-\frac{3\sqrt{3}}{2}\cdot\frac{a\,c_{\delta}\nu}{c\,(a(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}.
``` |  |

Now take any $`x\in\mathcal{I}(a,\nu)`$ and write it as
$`x=\frac{c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}{a}+\big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}{a}\big)\,y`$
with $`y\in\big(y_{-}(a,\nu),y_{+}(a,\nu)\big)`$. Iterating the
conjugacy identity gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
F^{\circ t}(x;a,\nu)=\frac{c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}{a}+\left(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}{a}\right)\,g_{a,\nu}^{\circ t}(y),\qquad\forall\,t\geq 0,
``` |  |

so $`g_{a,\nu}^{\circ t}(y)\in\big(y_{-}(a,\nu),y_{+}(a,\nu)\big)`$
implies $`F^{\circ t}(x;a,\nu)\in\mathcal{I}(a,\nu)`$, and
$`g_{a,\nu}^{\circ(t+1)}(y)>g_{a,\nu}^{\circ t}(y)`$ implies
$`F^{\circ(t+1)}(x;a,\nu)>F^{\circ t}(x;a,\nu)`$.

For the interval length, using the lemma’s bound,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle|\mathcal{I}(a,\nu)|=x_{+}(a,\nu)-x_{-}(a,\nu)`$ | $`\displaystyle=\left(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}{a}\right)\big(y_{+}(a,\nu)-y_{-}(a,\nu)\big)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq\left(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}{a}\right)\left(1-\frac{3\sqrt{3}}{2}\cdot\frac{a\,c_{\delta}\nu}{c\,(a(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}\right)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\left(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}{a}\right)-\frac{3\sqrt{3}}{2}\cdot\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{\,a(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\,}}.`$ |  |

For any parameters $`(a,\nu)`$ in the validity range of
Definition [A.1](#A1.Thmtheorem1 "Definition A.1 (ℐ(𝑎,𝜈)). ‣ A.2 Deferred Definitions and Explicit Expressions ‣ A. Notation and Deferred Definitions ‣ Appendix: Proofs ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula"),
recall that the endpoints $`x_{-}(a,\nu)<x_{+}(a,\nu)`$ are the two
fixed points of $`F(\cdot;a,\nu)`$, i.e.,

|     |                                                 |     |
|-----|-------------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       F\big(x_{\pm}(a,\nu);a,\nu\big)=x_{\pm}(a,\nu).
       ```                                              |     |

Define the fixed-point equation

|     |                                |     |
|-----|--------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       \Phi(x,a,\nu):=x-F(x;a,\nu)=0.
       ```                             |     |

Whenever $`\partial_{x}\Phi\big(x_{\pm}(a,\nu),a,\nu\big)\neq 0`$, the
implicit function theorem gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{\partial}{\partial a}x_{\pm}(a,\nu)=-\frac{\partial_{a}\Phi\big(x_{\pm}(a,\nu),a,\nu\big)}{\partial_{x}\Phi\big(x_{\pm}(a,\nu),a,\nu\big)},\qquad\frac{\partial}{\partial\nu}x_{\pm}(a,\nu)=-\frac{\partial_{\nu}\Phi\big(x_{\pm}(a,\nu),a,\nu\big)}{\partial_{x}\Phi\big(x_{\pm}(a,\nu),a,\nu\big)}.
``` |  |

Fix $`\nu`$ and view $`\Phi`$ as a function of $`(a,x)`$. First, for any
$`x>c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu/a`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\partial_{a}\Phi(x,a,\nu)=-\partial_{a}F(x;a,\nu)=-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c}\cdot\frac{x}{2\,(ax-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}\;<\;0.
``` |  |

Next, $`\partial_{x}\Phi(x,a,\nu)=1-\partial_{x}F(x;a,\nu)`$. To
determine its sign at the fixed points, consider the affine map

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
x=\frac{c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}{a}+\left(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}{a}\right)y,
``` |  |

which maps $`F(x;a,\nu)`$ to $`g_{a,\nu}(y)=1-\sigma(a,\nu)/\sqrt{y}`$.
At a fixed point $`y=g_{a,\nu}(y)`$ we have
$`\sigma(a,\nu)=(1-y)\sqrt{y}`$, hence

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
g^{\prime}_{a,\nu}(y)=\frac{\sigma(a,\nu)}{2y^{3/2}}=\frac{1-y}{2y}.
``` |  |

Since $`y_{-}(a,\nu)\in(0,1/3)`$ and $`y_{+}(a,\nu)\in(1/3,1)`$,
$`g^{\prime}_{a,\nu}\big(y_{-}(a,\nu)\big)>1`$ and
$`g^{\prime}_{a,\nu}\big(y_{+}(a,\nu)\big)<1.`$ Therefore
$`\partial_{x}\Phi\big(x_{-}(a,\nu),a,\nu\big)<0`$ and
$`\partial_{x}\Phi\big(x_{+}(a,\nu),a,\nu\big)>0.`$ Combining with
$`\partial_{a}\Phi(x_{\pm}(a,\nu),a,\nu)<0`$ yields

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{\partial}{\partial a}x_{-}(a,\nu)<0,\qquad\frac{\partial}{\partial a}x_{+}(a,\nu)>0.
``` |  |

Consequently, for any $`a_{2}>a_{1}`$ (with the same fixed $`\nu`$),

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
x_{-}(a_{2},\nu)<x_{-}(a_{1},\nu),\qquad x_{+}(a_{2},\nu)>x_{+}(a_{1},\nu),
``` |  |

i.e., $`\mathcal{I}(a_{1},\nu)\subset\mathcal{I}(a_{2},\nu)`$.

Finally, fix $`a`$ and view $`\Phi`$ as a function of $`(\nu,x)`$. For
any $`x>c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu/a`$,
$`\partial_{\nu}\Phi(x,a,\nu)=-\partial_{\nu}F(x;a,\nu).`$ A direct
differentiation shows $`\partial_{\nu}F(x;a,\nu)<0`$, hence
$`\partial_{\nu}\Phi(x,a,\nu)>0.`$ Moreover, as established above,
$`\partial_{x}\Phi\big(x_{-}(a,\nu),a,\nu\big)<0`$ and
$`\partial_{x}\Phi\big(x_{+}(a,\nu),a,\nu\big)>0.`$ Therefore,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{\partial}{\partial\nu}x_{-}(a,\nu)>0,\qquad\frac{\partial}{\partial\nu}x_{+}(a,\nu)<0,
``` |  |

Consequently, for any $`\nu_{2}>\nu_{1}`$ (with the same fixed $`a`$).

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
x_{-}(a,\nu_{2})>x_{-}(a,\nu_{1}),\qquad x_{+}(a,\nu_{2})<x_{+}(a,\nu_{1}),
``` |  |

which implies $`\mathcal{I}(a,\nu_{2})\subset\mathcal{I}(a,\nu_{1})`$. ∎

### C.  Proofs for Section [5](#S5 "5 Iterative Easy-to-Hard Curriculum for Self-Improvement ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula")

#### C.1  Proof of Theorem [5.2](#S5.Thmtheorem2 "Theorem 5.2. ‣ 5.2 Main Results ‣ 5 Iterative Easy-to-Hard Curriculum for Self-Improvement ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula")

###### Proof.

We now prove the first part of the theorem. By
Definition [A.2](#A1.Thmtheorem2 "Definition A.2 ({ℳ_𝑖}_{𝑖=1}^4 and 𝒩). ‣ A.2 Deferred Definitions and Explicit Expressions ‣ A. Notation and Deferred Definitions ‣ Appendix: Proofs ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula"),
the conditions
$`\mathcal{M}_{i}\big(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})\big)<0`$
for all $`i\in[4]`$ are equivalent to requiring that both
$`V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})`$ and
$`1-\gamma-c_{\delta}\nu/(c\sqrt{a_{0}V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu})`$
lie in the open interval
$`(x_{-}(2^{-\beta},\nu),\,x_{+}(2^{-\beta},\nu))`$. By
Corollary [B.3](#A2.Thmtheorem3 "Corollary B.3. ‣ B.3 Proof of Proposition ‣ B. Proofs for Section ‣ Appendix: Proofs ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula"),
since $`2^{-\beta}<1`$, we have
$`V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})\in\mathcal{I}(2^{-\beta},\nu)\subset\mathcal{I}(1,\nu)`$.
Therefore, by
Corollary [4.4](#S4.Thmtheorem4 "Corollary 4.4. ‣ 4.2 Multi-Step Self-Improvement ‣ 4 Iterative Self-Improvement ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula")
and
Proposition [4.5](#S4.Thmtheorem5 "Proposition 4.5. ‣ 4.2 Multi-Step Self-Improvement ‣ 4 Iterative Self-Improvement ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula"),
it follows that, with high probability,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V_{p_{0}}\big(\hat{\theta}^{\mathrm{B}}_{L}\big)\;\geq\;F^{\circ L}\Big(V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})\Big),
``` |  |

and the sequence
$`\{F^{\circ t}(V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0}))\}_{t\geq 0}`$ is
monotonically increasing in $`t`$, where

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
F(x)\;=\;1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{x-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}.
``` |  |

Next, recall that $`\hat{\theta}^{\mathrm{E2H}}_{0}=\hat{\theta}_{0}`$
and for easy-to-hard, during iteration $`t`$ we use distribution
$`p_{t+1}`$. By
Assumption [5.1](#S5.Thmtheorem1 "Assumption 5.1. ‣ Difficulty levels. ‣ 5.1 Easy-to-Hard Curriculum and Baseline ‣ 5 Iterative Easy-to-Hard Curriculum for Self-Improvement ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula"),
for every $`i\in[L-1]`$ and every $`\theta`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{V_{p_{i}}(\theta)}{V_{p_{i+1}}(\theta)}\;\geq\;\frac{i^{-\beta^{\prime}}}{(i+1)^{-\beta^{\prime}}}.
``` |  |

Equivalently,
$`V_{p_{i+1}}(\theta)\leq(i/(i+1))^{\beta^{\prime}}V_{p_{i}}(\theta)`$.
Iterating from $`1`$ to $`i-1`$ yields,
$`V_{p_{i}}(\theta)\leq i^{-\beta^{\prime}}\,V_{p_{1}}(\theta).`$
Applying this with $`\theta=\hat{\theta}_{0}`$ and averaging over $`i`$
gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})\;=\;\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{L}V_{p_{i}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})\;\leq\;\frac{1}{L}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{L}i^{-\beta^{\prime}}\Big)\,V_{p_{1}}(\hat{\theta}_{0}),
``` |  |

hence

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V_{p_{1}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})\;\geq\;a_{0}\,V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0}),\qquad a_{0}\;:=\;\frac{L}{\sum_{i=1}^{L}i^{-\beta^{\prime}}}.
``` |  |

Note that since $`i^{-\beta^{\prime}}<1`$ for all $`i\geq 2`$, we have
$`\sum_{i=1}^{L}i^{-\beta^{\prime}}<L`$ (for $`L\geq 2`$), and therefore
$`a_{0}>1`$.

Then, at iteration $`t`$, easy-to-hard trains on $`p_{t+1}`$. By
Corollary [4.4](#S4.Thmtheorem4 "Corollary 4.4. ‣ 4.2 Multi-Step Self-Improvement ‣ 4 Iterative Self-Improvement ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula")
(substitute $`p_{0}`$ with $`p_{t+1}`$), with high probability,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V_{p_{t+1}}\!\big(\hat{\theta}^{\mathrm{E2H}}_{t+1}\big)\;\geq\;F\Big(V_{p_{t+1}}\!\big(\hat{\theta}^{\mathrm{E2H}}_{t}\big)\Big),\qquad t=0,1,\ldots,L-1.
``` |  |

Again by
Assumption [5.1](#S5.Thmtheorem1 "Assumption 5.1. ‣ Difficulty levels. ‣ 5.1 Easy-to-Hard Curriculum and Baseline ‣ 5 Iterative Easy-to-Hard Curriculum for Self-Improvement ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula"),
for every $`t\in[L-1]`$ and every $`\theta\in\Theta`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{V_{p_{t}}(\theta)}{V_{p_{t+1}}(\theta)}\;\leq\;\frac{t^{-\beta}}{(t+1)^{-\beta}},
``` |  |

equivalently,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V_{p_{t+1}}(\theta)\;\geq\;a_{t}\,V_{p_{t}}(\theta),\qquad a_{t}\;:=\;\frac{(t+1)^{-\beta}}{t^{-\beta}}\;<\;1.
``` |  |

Define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
H_{t}(x)\;:=\;F(a_{t}x)\;=\;1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{t}x-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}},\qquad t=0,1,\ldots,L-1,
``` |  |

with $`\{a_{t}\}`$ defined above, We now chain the previous steps.
First, since
$`V_{p_{1}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})\geq a_{0}V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})`$, we
have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V_{p_{1}}\!\big(\hat{\theta}^{\mathrm{E2H}}_{1}\big)\;\geq\;F\Big(V_{p_{1}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})\Big)\;\geq\;F\Big(a_{0}V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})\Big)\;=\;H_{0}\Big(V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})\Big).
``` |  |

Next, for $`t=1`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V_{p_{2}}\!\big(\hat{\theta}^{\mathrm{E2H}}_{2}\big)\;\geq\;F\Big(V_{p_{2}}(\hat{\theta}^{\mathrm{E2H}}_{1})\Big)\;\geq\;F\Big(a_{1}V_{p_{1}}(\hat{\theta}^{\mathrm{E2H}}_{1})\Big)\;=\;H_{1}\Big(V_{p_{1}}(\hat{\theta}^{\mathrm{E2H}}_{1})\Big).
``` |  |

Continuing this argument inductively for $`t=2,\ldots,L-1`$ yields the
recursion

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V_{p_{t+1}}\!\big(\hat{\theta}^{\mathrm{E2H}}_{t+1}\big)\;\geq\;H_{t}\Big(V_{p_{t}}(\hat{\theta}^{\mathrm{E2H}}_{t})\Big),\qquad t=0,1,\ldots,L-1,
``` |  |

and therefore, after $`L`$ steps,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V_{p_{L}}\!\big(\hat{\theta}^{\mathrm{E2H}}_{L}\big)\;\geq\;(H_{L-1}\circ H_{L-2}\circ\cdots\circ H_{0})\Big(V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})\Big).
``` |  |

We finally lower bound $`V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}^{\mathrm{E2H}}_{L})`$ in
terms of $`V_{p_{L}}(\hat{\theta}^{\mathrm{E2H}}_{L})`$. Using
Assumption [5.1](#S5.Thmtheorem1 "Assumption 5.1. ‣ Difficulty levels. ‣ 5.1 Easy-to-Hard Curriculum and Baseline ‣ 5 Iterative Easy-to-Hard Curriculum for Self-Improvement ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula")
and iterating as in for $`a_{0}`$, for every $`i\in\{1,\ldots,L\}`$ and
every $`\theta\in\Theta`$ we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V_{p_{i}}(\theta)\;\geq\;\Big(\frac{i^{-\beta^{\prime}}}{L^{-\beta^{\prime}}}\Big)\,V_{p_{L}}(\theta)\;=\;\Big(\frac{L}{i}\Big)^{\beta^{\prime}}V_{p_{L}}(\theta).
``` |  |

Averaging over $`i`$ gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V_{p_{0}}(\theta)\;=\;\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{L}V_{p_{i}}(\theta)\;\geq\;\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{L}\Big(\frac{L}{i}\Big)^{\beta^{\prime}}V_{p_{L}}(\theta)\;=\;\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{L}i^{-\beta^{\prime}}}{L^{1-\beta^{\prime}}}\,V_{p_{L}}(\theta).
``` |  |

Applying this with $`\theta=\hat{\theta}^{\mathrm{E2H}}_{L}`$ yields

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V_{p_{0}}\big(\hat{\theta}^{\mathrm{E2H}}_{L}\big)\;\geq\;a_{L}\,V_{p_{L}}\!\big(\hat{\theta}^{\mathrm{E2H}}_{L}\big),\qquad a_{L}\;:=\;\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{L}i^{-\beta^{\prime}}}{L^{1-\beta^{\prime}}},
``` |  |

and clearly $`a_{L}>1`$ since
$`\sum_{i=1}^{L}i^{-\beta^{\prime}}>L\cdot L^{-\beta^{\prime}}=L^{1-\beta^{\prime}}`$.
Defining $`G(x):=a_{L}x`$, we obtain

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V_{p_{0}}\big(\hat{\theta}^{\mathrm{E2H}}_{L}\big)\;\geq\;(G\circ H_{L-1}\circ H_{L-2}\circ\cdots\circ H_{0})\Big(V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})\Big),
``` |  |

as claimed. Moreover, note that for $`t\in[L-1]`$, $`a_{t}`$ is strictly
increasing in $`t`$. Therefore, by
Corollary [B.3](#A2.Thmtheorem3 "Corollary B.3. ‣ B.3 Proof of Proposition ‣ B. Proofs for Section ‣ Appendix: Proofs ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula"),
the associated intervals $`\mathcal{I}(a_{t},\nu)`$ expand as $`t`$
increases. Since $`V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})`$ and
$`H_{t}(V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0}))=1-\gamma-c_{\delta}\nu/(c\sqrt{a_{0}V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu})`$
lies in the smallest admissible interval
$`(x_{-}(2^{-\beta},\nu),\,x_{+}(2^{-\beta},\nu))`$, the chained lower
bounds stay within the corresponding invariant intervals and
satisfy$`\{(H_{t}\circ H_{t-1}\circ\cdots\circ H_{0})\big(V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})\big)\}_{t\geq 0}`$
is monotonically increasing in $`t`$.

We now prove the second part of the theorem. Although the statement of
Theorem [5.2](#S5.Thmtheorem2 "Theorem 5.2. ‣ 5.2 Main Results ‣ 5 Iterative Easy-to-Hard Curriculum for Self-Improvement ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula")
concerns $`\nu>0`$, the maps involved are continuous in $`\nu`$, and it
is convenient to first analyze the case $`\nu=0`$. To distinguish the
dependence on $`\nu`$, we write the baseline map as $`F_{\nu}(\cdot)`$
and the easy-to-hard maps as $`H_{t,\nu}(\cdot)`$.

When $`\nu=0`$, the maps simplify to constants:

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
F_{0}(x)\;=\;1-\gamma,\qquad H_{t,0}(x)\;=\;1-\gamma,\qquad t=0,1,\ldots,L-1,\qquad G(x)=a_{L}x.
``` |  |

Define the comparison gap

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Delta(\nu,x)\;:=\;\big(G\circ H_{L-1,\nu}\circ H_{L-2,\nu}\circ\cdots\circ H_{0,\nu}\big)(x)\;-\;F_{\nu}^{\circ L}(x).
``` |  |

Then we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Delta(0,x)=G\big(H_{L-1,0}\circ\cdots\circ H_{0,0}(x)\big)-F_{0}^{\circ L}(x)=a_{L}(1-\gamma)-(1-\gamma)=(a_{L}-1)(1-\gamma).
``` |  |

Since $`a_{L}>1`$ and $`1-\gamma>0`$, it follows that
$`\Delta\big(0,V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})\big)=(a_{L}-1)(1-\gamma)\;>\;0.`$
Therefore, our next step is to lower bound
$`\Delta\big(\nu,V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})\big)-\Delta\big(0,V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})\big).`$
For convenience, denote $`x_{0}:=V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0}).`$⁴⁴ 4 We
will use this shorthand throughout the remainder of this proof (and in
subsequent proofs) whenever the dependence on the initialization is
through $`V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})`$. Observe that the above
difference involves two scalar sequences induced by the iterated maps.
For baseline, define $`\{x_{t}^{\mathrm{B}}(\nu)\}_{t=0}^{L}`$ by

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
x_{0}^{\mathrm{B}}(\nu)=x_{0},\qquad x_{t+1}^{\mathrm{B}}(\nu)=F_{\nu}\big(x_{t}^{\mathrm{B}}(\nu)\big),\qquad t=0,1,\ldots,L-1.
``` |  |

By construction,
$`F_{\nu}^{\circ L}(x_{0})\;=\;x_{L}^{\mathrm{B}}(\nu).`$ Similarly, for
easy-to-hard define $`\{x_{t}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)\}_{t=0}^{L}`$ by

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
x_{0}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)=x_{0},\qquad x_{t+1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)=H_{t,\nu}\big(x_{t}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)\big),\qquad t=0,1,\ldots,L-1.
``` |  |

By construction,
$`\big(G\circ H_{L-1,\nu}\circ H_{L-2,\nu}\circ\cdots\circ H_{0,\nu}\big)(x_{0})\;=\;a_{L}\,x_{L}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu).`$

Combining the above identities, we obtain

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Delta(\nu,x_{0})-\Delta(0,x_{0})`$ | $`\displaystyle=\Big(a_{L}x_{L}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)-x_{L}^{\mathrm{B}}(\nu)\Big)-\Big(a_{L}x_{L}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(0)-x_{L}^{\mathrm{B}}(0)\Big)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\big(x_{L}^{\mathrm{B}}(0)-x_{L}^{\mathrm{B}}(\nu)\big)-a_{L}\big(x_{L}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(0)-x_{L}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)\big).`$ |  |

Therefore, if we define the deviation sequences

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
e_{t}^{\mathrm{B}}(\nu)\;:=\;x_{t}^{\mathrm{B}}(0)-x_{t}^{\mathrm{B}}(\nu),\qquad e_{t}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)\;:=\;x_{t}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(0)-x_{t}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu),
``` |  |

then the quantity of interest can be written succinctly as
$`\Delta(\nu,x_{0})-\Delta(0,x_{0})=e_{L}^{\mathrm{B}}(\nu)-a_{L}\,e_{L}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu).`$
Hence, it suffices to derive a lower bound on
$`e_{L}^{\mathrm{B}}(\nu)`$ and an upper bound on
$`e_{L}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)`$.

For the lower bound on $`e_{L}^{\mathrm{B}}(\nu)`$, ignoring the trivial
case $`t=0`$, we start from

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
e_{1}^{\mathrm{B}}(\nu)=F_{0}(x_{0})-F_{\nu}(x_{0})=\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}.
``` |  |

More generally, for every $`t\geq 1`$ we can write

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle e_{t}^{\mathrm{B}}(\nu)`$ | $`\displaystyle=F_{0}\big(x_{t-1}^{\mathrm{B}}(0)\big)-F_{\nu}\big(x_{t-1}^{\mathrm{B}}(\nu)\big)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\Big(F_{0}\big(x_{t-1}^{\mathrm{B}}(0)\big)-F_{\nu}\big(x_{t-1}^{\mathrm{B}}(0)\big)\Big)+\Big(F_{\nu}\big(x_{t-1}^{\mathrm{B}}(0)\big)-F_{\nu}\big(x_{t-1}^{\mathrm{B}}(\nu)\big)\Big).`$ |  |

The first term is nonnegative because
$`F_{\nu}(x)\leq F_{0}(x)=1-\gamma`$ for all admissible $`x`$ when
$`\nu>0`$. The second term is also nonnegative because $`F_{\nu}`$ is
increasing and
$`x_{t-1}^{\mathrm{B}}(0)\geq x_{t-1}^{\mathrm{B}}(\nu)`$. Hence,

|     |                                                      |     |
|-----|------------------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       e_{t}^{\mathrm{B}}(\nu)\geq 0,\qquad\forall t\geq 1.
       ```                                                   |     |

Next we derive a quantitative lower bound. The first difference is
explicit:

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
F_{0}\big(x_{t-1}^{\mathrm{B}}(0)\big)-F_{\nu}\big(x_{t-1}^{\mathrm{B}}(0)\big)=\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{\,1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\,}}.
``` |  |

For the second difference, note that

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
F_{\nu}^{\prime}(x)=\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(x-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}},
``` |  |

which is monotonically decreasing in $`x`$ over its domain. By the mean
value theorem, there exists
$`\xi_{t-1}\in\big[x_{t-1}^{\mathrm{B}}(\nu),\,1-\gamma\big]`$ such that
$`F_{\nu}\big(x_{t-1}^{\mathrm{B}}(0)\big)-F_{\nu}\big(x_{t-1}^{\mathrm{B}}(\nu)\big)=F_{\nu}^{\prime}(\xi_{t-1})\,e_{t-1}^{\mathrm{B}}(\nu).`$
Since $`F_{\nu}^{\prime}`$ is decreasing and $`\xi_{t-1}\leq 1-\gamma`$,
we have $`F_{\nu}^{\prime}(\xi_{t-1})\geq F_{\nu}^{\prime}(1-\gamma)`$,
and thus

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
F_{\nu}\big(x_{t-1}^{\mathrm{B}}(0)\big)-F_{\nu}\big(x_{t-1}^{\mathrm{B}}(\nu)\big)\;\geq\;\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}\;e_{t-1}^{\mathrm{B}}(\nu).
``` |  |

Combining the two pieces yields the recursion: for every
$`t=2,3,\ldots,L`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
e_{t}^{\mathrm{B}}(\nu)\;\geq\;\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{\,1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\,}}\;+\;\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}\;e_{t-1}^{\mathrm{B}}(\nu).
``` |  |

Iterating from $`t=2`$ up to $`t=L`$ gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
e_{L}^{\mathrm{B}}(\nu)\;\geq\;\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{\,1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\,}}\cdot\frac{1-\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!L-1}}{1-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}}\;+\;\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!L-1}\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}.
``` |  |

For the upper bound on $`e_{L}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)`$, ignoring the
trivial case $`t=0`$, we start from

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
e_{1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)=H_{0,0}(x_{0})-H_{0,\nu}(x_{0})=\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}.
``` |  |

More generally, for every $`t\geq 1`$ we can write

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle e_{t}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)`$ | $`\displaystyle=H_{t-1,0}\big(x_{t-1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(0)\big)-H_{t-1,\nu}\big(x_{t-1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)\big)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\Big(H_{t-1,0}\big(x_{t-1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(0)\big)-H_{t-1,\nu}\big(x_{t-1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(0)\big)\Big)+\Big(H_{t-1,\nu}\big(x_{t-1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(0)\big)-H_{t-1,\nu}\big(x_{t-1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)\big)\Big).`$ |  |

Similarly, these two terms are nonnegative. Hence,
$`e_{t}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)\geq 0`$, $`\forall t\geq 1.`$

Next we derive a quantitative upper bound. For the first difference,
note that $`x_{t-1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(0)=1-\gamma`$ for all $`t\geq 1`$,
and thus

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
H_{t-1,0}\big(x_{t-1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(0)\big)-H_{t-1,\nu}\big(x_{t-1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(0)\big)=\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{t-1}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}.
``` |  |

For the second difference, we compute the derivative

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
H_{t-1,\nu}^{\prime}(x)=\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c}\cdot\frac{a_{t-1}}{(a_{t-1}x-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}},
``` |  |

which is monotonically decreasing in $`x`$ over its domain. By the mean
value theorem, there exists
$`\zeta_{t-1}\in\big[x_{t-1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu),\,1-\gamma\big]`$ such
that
$`H_{t-1,\nu}\big(x_{t-1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(0)\big)-H_{t-1,\nu}\big(x_{t-1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)\big)=H_{t-1,\nu}^{\prime}(\zeta_{t-1})\,e_{t-1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu).`$
Since $`H_{t-1,\nu}^{\prime}`$ is decreasing and
$`\zeta_{t-1}\geq x_{t-1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)`$, we have
$`H_{t-1,\nu}^{\prime}(\zeta_{t-1})\leq H_{t-1,\nu}^{\prime}(x_{t-1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu))`$.
Moreover, since $`\{x_{t}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)\}_{t\geq 0}`$ is
monotonically increasing and

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
x_{1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)=H_{0,\nu}(x_{0})=1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}},
``` |  |

we have $`x_{t-1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)\geq x_{1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)`$
for all $`t\geq 2`$. Using again that $`H_{t-1,\nu}^{\prime}`$ is
decreasing, it follows that for all $`t\geq 2`$,
$`H_{t-1,\nu}^{\prime}(x_{t-1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu))\leq H_{t-1,\nu}^{\prime}\big(x_{1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)\big).`$
Combining the above displays yields, for every $`t=2,3,\ldots,L`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
H_{t-1,\nu}\big(x_{t-1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(0)\big)-H_{t-1,\nu}\big(x_{t-1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)\big)\;\leq\;H_{t-1,\nu}^{\prime}\big(x_{1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)\big)\;e_{t-1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu),
``` |  |

where

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
H_{t-1,\nu}^{\prime}\big(x_{1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)\big)=\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c}\cdot\frac{a_{t-1}}{\Big(a_{t-1}x_{1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}.
``` |  |

Therefore, combining the two pieces, we obtain the recursion: for every
$`t=2,3,\ldots,L`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
e_{t}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)\;\leq\;\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{t-1}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\;+\;\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c}\cdot\frac{a_{t-1}}{\Big(a_{t-1}x_{1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\;e_{t-1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu).
``` |  |

Unrolling gives

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle e_{L}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{L-1}\left(\prod_{s=j+1}^{L-1}\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c}\cdot\frac{a_{s}}{\Big(a_{s}x_{1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\right)\cdot\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{j}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\left(\prod_{s=1}^{L-1}\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c}\cdot\frac{a_{s}}{\Big(a_{s}x_{1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\right)\cdot\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}.`$ |  |

Since $`a_{t}=(t/(t+1))^{\beta}`$ is increasing in $`t`$ for
$`t\geq 1`$, we have $`a_{t}\geq a_{1}=2^{-\beta}`$ for all
$`t\in[L-1]`$. Hence, for every $`j\in[L-1]`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{1}{\sqrt{a_{j}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\;\leq\;\frac{1}{\sqrt{2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}},
``` |  |

and for every $`s\in[L-1]`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{1}{\Big(a_{s}x_{1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\;\leq\;\frac{1}{\Big(2^{-\beta}x_{1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}.
``` |  |

Applying these bounds to the unrolled expression yields

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle e_{L}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\sum_{j=1}^{L-1}\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}x_{1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!L-1-j}\left(\prod_{s=j+1}^{L-1}a_{s}\right)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}x_{1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!L-1}\left(\prod_{s=1}^{L-1}a_{s}\right)\cdot\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}.`$ |  |

Next, For $`0\leq j\leq L-1`$,
$`\prod_{s=j+1}^{L-1}a_{s}=\prod_{s=j+1}^{L-1}\Big(\frac{s}{s+1}\Big)^{\beta}=\Big(\frac{j+1}{L}\Big)^{\beta}.`$
Substituting these identities gives

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle e_{L}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\sum_{j=1}^{L-1}\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}x_{1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!L-1-j}\Big(\frac{j+1}{L}\Big)^{\beta}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}x_{1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!L-1}L^{-\beta}\cdot\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\sum_{m=0}^{L-2}\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}x_{1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!m}\Big(1-\frac{m}{L}\Big)^{\beta}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}x_{1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!L-1}L^{-\beta}\cdot\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}.`$ |  |

Finally, since for $`m\in\{0,1,\ldots,L-2\}`$,
$`(1-\frac{m}{L})^{\beta}\leq e^{-\frac{\beta}{L}m},`$

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle e_{L}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\sum_{m=0}^{L-2}\left[\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}x_{1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\cdot e^{-\beta/L}\right]^{\!m}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}x_{1}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!L-1}L^{-\beta}\cdot\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\cdot\frac{1-\left[\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\cdot e^{-\beta/L}\right]^{\!L-1}}{1-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\cdot e^{-\beta/L}}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!L-1}L^{-\beta}\cdot\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}.`$ |  |

Therefore, given
$`\Delta(\nu,x_{0})-\Delta(0,x_{0})\;=\;e_{L}^{\mathrm{B}}(\nu)-a_{L}\,e_{L}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu),`$
combining the lower bound on $`e_{L}^{\mathrm{B}}(\nu)`$ and the upper
bound on $`e_{L}^{\mathrm{E2H}}(\nu)`$ yields

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\quad\Delta(\nu,x_{0})-\Delta(0,x_{0})`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq\Bigg[\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{\,1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\,}}\cdot\frac{1-\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!L-1}}{1-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}}+\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!L-1}\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Bigg]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt-a_{L}\Bigg[\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\cdot\frac{1-\left[\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\cdot e^{-\beta/L}\right]^{\!L-1}}{1-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\cdot e^{-\beta/L}}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt+\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!L-1}L^{-\beta}\cdot\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Bigg]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle>\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{\,1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\,}}\cdot\frac{1-\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!L-1}}{1-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt-a_{L}\Bigg[\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\cdot\frac{1}{1-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\cdot e^{-\beta/L}}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt+\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!L-1}L^{-\beta}\cdot\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Bigg]`$ |  |

When the derived lower bound is greater than
$`-\frac{1}{2}\,\Delta(0,x_{0})=-\frac{1}{2}\,(a_{L}-1)(1-\gamma),`$
then it follows immediately that

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Delta(\nu,x_{0})=\Delta(0,x_{0})+\big(\Delta(\nu,x_{0})-\Delta(0,x_{0})\big)\;>\;\frac{1}{2}\,(a_{L}-1)(1-\gamma)\;>\;0.
``` |  |

Therefore, we define the corresponding constraint by
$`\mathcal{N}\big(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})\big)<0`$
as in
Definition [A.2](#A1.Thmtheorem2 "Definition A.2 ({ℳ_𝑖}_{𝑖=1}^4 and 𝒩). ‣ A.2 Deferred Definitions and Explicit Expressions ‣ A. Notation and Deferred Definitions ‣ Appendix: Proofs ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula"),
under this constraint, we conclude that

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\big(G\circ H_{L-1,\nu}\circ\cdots\circ H_{0,\nu}\big)(x_{0})\;>\;F_{\nu}^{\circ L}(x_{0}),
``` |  |

i.e., the easy-to-hard lower bound is strictly larger than the baseline
lower bound. ∎

#### C.2  Proof of Corollary [5.3](#S5.Thmtheorem3 "Corollary 5.3. ‣ Interpreting {ℳ_𝑖<0}. ‣ 5.2 Main Results ‣ 5 Iterative Easy-to-Hard Curriculum for Self-Improvement ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula")

###### Proof.

Let

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
H_{0}(V)\;:=\;1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}V-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}},
``` |  |

which is strictly increasing in $`V`$. Then, the inequality
$`x_{-}(2^{-\beta},\nu)<H_{0}\big(V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})\big)<x_{+}(2^{-\beta},\nu)`$
is equivalent to $`V_{-}(\nu)<V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})<V_{+}(\nu),`$
where $`V_{\pm}(\nu)`$ are the unique solutions to
$`H_{0}(V)=x_{\pm}(2^{-\beta},\nu)`$, respectively. Solving this gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\sqrt{a_{0}V_{\pm}(\nu)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}=\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\,(1-\gamma-x_{\pm}(2^{-\beta},\nu))}.
``` |  |

Moreover, using the fact that $`x_{\pm}(2^{-\beta},\nu)`$ are the fixed
points, i.e.,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
x_{\pm}(2^{-\beta},\nu)=1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{\,2^{-\beta}x_{\pm}(2^{-\beta},\nu)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\,}},
``` |  |

we obtain

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\,(1-\gamma-x_{\pm}(2^{-\beta},\nu))}=\sqrt{\,2^{-\beta}x_{\pm}(2^{-\beta},\nu)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\,}.
``` |  |

Therefore,
$`V_{\pm}(\nu)=\frac{2^{-\beta}}{a_{0}}\,x_{\pm}(2^{-\beta},\nu).`$
Consequently, the second constraint is equivalent to

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{2^{-\beta}}{a_{0}}x_{-}(2^{-\beta},\nu)<V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})<\frac{2^{-\beta}}{a_{0}}x_{+}(2^{-\beta},\nu).
``` |  |

Intersecting with the first constraint
$`x_{-}(2^{-\beta},\nu)<V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})<x_{+}(2^{-\beta},\nu)`$
yields that the feasible set of $`V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})`$ is

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{M}}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu)=\Big(x_{-}(2^{-\beta},\nu),\,\frac{2^{-\beta}}{a_{0}}x_{+}(2^{-\beta},\nu)\Big).
``` |  |

For the interval length
$`\big|\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{M}}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu)\big|`$, we
have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\big|\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{M}}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu)\big|`$ | $`\displaystyle=\big(x_{+}(2^{-\beta},\nu)-x_{-}(2^{-\beta},\nu)\big)-\Big(1-\frac{2^{-\beta}}{a_{0}}\Big)x_{+}(2^{-\beta},\nu)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq\big(x_{+}(2^{-\beta},\nu)-x_{-}(2^{-\beta},\nu)\big)-\Big(1-\frac{2^{-\beta}}{a_{0}}\Big)(1-\gamma).`$ |  |

Applying
Corollary [B.3](#A2.Thmtheorem3 "Corollary B.3. ‣ B.3 Proof of Proposition ‣ B. Proofs for Section ‣ Appendix: Proofs ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula")
with parameter $`2^{-\beta}`$ gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\big|\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{M}}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu)\big|\;\geq\;\frac{2^{-\beta}}{a_{0}}(1-\gamma)-2^{\beta}c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu-\frac{3\sqrt{3}}{2}\cdot\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{\,2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\,}}.
``` |  |

By
Corollary [B.3](#A2.Thmtheorem3 "Corollary B.3. ‣ B.3 Proof of Proposition ‣ B. Proofs for Section ‣ Appendix: Proofs ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula"),
we have $`x_{-}(2^{-\beta},0)=0`$ and $`x_{+}(2^{-\beta},0)=1-\gamma`$,
hence
$`\big|\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{M}}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,0)\big|=\frac{2^{-\beta}}{a_{0}}(1-\gamma).`$
Therefore,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\big|\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{M}}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,0)\big|-\big|\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{M}}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu)\big|\;\leq\;2^{\beta}c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu+\frac{3\sqrt{3}}{2}\cdot\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{\,2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\,}}.
``` |  |

Moreover,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\big|\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{M}}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,0)\big|-\big|\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{M}}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu)\big|`$ | $`\displaystyle=\frac{2^{-\beta}}{a_{0}}(1-\gamma)-\left(\frac{2^{-\beta}}{a_{0}}x_{+}(2^{-\beta},\nu)-x_{-}(2^{-\beta},\nu)\right)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\frac{2^{-\beta}}{a_{0}}\big(1-\gamma-x_{+}(2^{-\beta},\nu)\big)+x_{-}(2^{-\beta},\nu).`$ |  |

Since $`x_{+}(2^{-\beta},\nu)<1-\gamma`$, the first term above is
nonnegative, hence
$`\big|\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{M}}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,0)\big|-\big|\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{M}}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu)\big|\geq x_{-}(2^{-\beta},\nu).`$
By the definition of $`x_{-}(2^{-\beta},\nu)`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
x_{-}(2^{-\beta},\nu)=2^{\beta}c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu+\Big(1-\gamma-2^{\beta}c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)\,y_{-}(\nu)\;\geq\;2^{\beta}c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu,
``` |  |

which completes the proof. ∎

#### C.3  Proof of Proposition [5.5](#S5.Thmtheorem5 "Proposition 5.5. ‣ Interpreting 𝒩<0. ‣ 5.2 Main Results ‣ 5 Iterative Easy-to-Hard Curriculum for Self-Improvement ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula")

###### Proof.

Proposition [5.5](#S5.Thmtheorem5 "Proposition 5.5. ‣ Interpreting 𝒩<0. ‣ 5.2 Main Results ‣ 5 Iterative Easy-to-Hard Curriculum for Self-Improvement ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula")
follows immediately by combining
Lemma [C.1](#A3.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma C.1. ‣ C.3 Proof of Proposition ‣ C. Proofs for Section ‣ Appendix: Proofs ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula"),
Lemma [C.2](#A3.Thmtheorem2 "Lemma C.2. ‣ C.3 Proof of Proposition ‣ C. Proofs for Section ‣ Appendix: Proofs ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula"),
and
Lemma [C.3](#A3.Thmtheorem3 "Lemma C.3. ‣ C.3 Proof of Proposition ‣ C. Proofs for Section ‣ Appendix: Proofs ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula").
∎

###### Lemma C.1.

Under the notation of
Proposition [5.5](#S5.Thmtheorem5 "Proposition 5.5. ‣ Interpreting 𝒩<0. ‣ 5.2 Main Results ‣ 5 Iterative Easy-to-Hard Curriculum for Self-Improvement ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula"),
for fixed $`(\beta^{\prime},\beta)`$, the length of the interval
$`\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{N}}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu)`$ for which
$`\mathcal{N}\big(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})\big)<0`$
is monotonically decreasing in $`\nu`$, and
$`\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{N}}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu)`$ can be written
in the form

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{N}}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu)=\bigl(x(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu),\,1-\gamma\bigr).
``` |  |

###### Proof.

Let $`x_{0}:=V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})`$ for convenience. First, we
show that the additional constraint
$`\mathcal{N}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x_{0})<0`$ induces an admissible
region that is monotonically decreasing in $`\nu`$.

Define

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x_{0}):=`$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{\,1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\,}}\cdot\frac{1-\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!L-1}}{1-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle-a_{L}\Bigg[\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\cdot\frac{1}{1-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\cdot e^{-\beta/L}}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!L-1}L^{-\beta}\cdot\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Bigg].`$ |  |

Then, by
Theorem [5.2](#S5.Thmtheorem2 "Theorem 5.2. ‣ 5.2 Main Results ‣ 5 Iterative Easy-to-Hard Curriculum for Self-Improvement ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula"),
$`\mathcal{N}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x_{0})<0`$ is equivalent to
$`\mathcal{E}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x_{0})>-\frac{1}{2}\,(a_{L}-1)(1-\gamma).`$
Moreover, the right-hand side above is a fixed constant since
$`\beta^{\prime}`$ is fixed.

Next, note that $`\mathcal{E}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x_{0})`$ is
monotonically increasing in $`x_{0}`$. Moreover, as $`x_{0}`$ decreases
to $`0`$, for denominators, the term

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
1-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\cdot e^{-\beta/L}
``` |  |

is the first to approach $`0`$, which implies that
$`\mathcal{E}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x_{0})\to-\infty`$. On the other
hand, when $`\nu=0`$ we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{E}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,0,x_{0})=0>-\frac{1}{2}\,(a_{L}-1)(1-\gamma)
``` |  |

for all $`x_{0}`$. Hence, by continuity, for sufficiently small
$`\nu>0`$, the set of $`x_{0}`$ satisfying
$`\mathcal{E}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x_{0})>-\frac{1}{2}\,(a_{L}-1)(1-\gamma)`$
is non-empty. Combining the above, as $`\nu`$ increases gradually from
$`0`$, the set of $`x_{0}`$ such that
$`\mathcal{N}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x_{0})<0`$ must lie in a
non-empty interval of the form

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{N}}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu)=\bigl(x(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu),\,1-\gamma\bigr).
``` |  |

Therefore, it suffices to show that the minimal admissible threshold
$`x(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu)`$ is monotonically increasing in $`\nu`$.
Define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Phi(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x)\;:=\;\mathcal{E}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x)+\frac{1}{2}\,(a_{L}-1)(1-\gamma),
``` |  |

By definition of $`x(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu)`$, we have
$`\Phi\big(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu)\big)=0.`$
The implicit function theorem implies that

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
x^{\prime}(\nu)=-\frac{\partial_{\nu}\Phi\big(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu)\big)}{\partial_{x}\Phi\big(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu)\big)}=-\frac{\partial_{\nu}\mathcal{E}\big(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu)\big)}{\partial_{x}\mathcal{E}\big(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu)\big)}.
``` |  |

Since $`\partial_{x}\mathcal{E}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x)>0`$, to show
$`x^{\prime}(\nu)>0`$, it suffices to show that
$`\mathcal{E}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x_{0})`$ is monotonically
decreasing in $`\nu`$.

Note first that the last term inside the brackets,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!L-1}L^{-\beta}\cdot\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}},
``` |  |

is increasing as $`\nu`$ increases. Hence, after multiplying by the
coefficient $`-a_{L}`$, this contribution is decreasing in $`\nu`$. It
therefore suffices to prove that the remaining part,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\quad\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{\,1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\,}}\cdot\frac{1-\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!L-1}}{1-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle-\;a_{L}\Bigg[\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\cdot\frac{1}{1-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\cdot e^{-\beta/L}}\Bigg].`$ |  |

has strictly negative derivative with respect to $`\nu`$.

To this end, it is enough to verify the following two comparison
statements: first, both the function value and the derivative (with
respect to $`\nu`$) of

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
U(\nu)=\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{\,1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\,}}
``` |  |

are strictly smaller than those of

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\widetilde{U}(\nu)=\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{\,2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\,}},
``` |  |

and second, both the function value and the derivative (with respect to
$`\nu`$) of

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V(\nu)=\frac{1-\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!L-1}}{1-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}}
``` |  |

are strictly smaller than those of

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\widetilde{V}(\nu)=\frac{1}{1-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\cdot e^{-\beta/L}}.
``` |  |

Indeed, by the product rule,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\nu}\Big(U(\nu)V(\nu)-a_{L}\,\widetilde{U}(\nu)\widetilde{V}(\nu)\Big)=\Big(U^{\prime}(\nu)V(\nu)+U(\nu)V^{\prime}(\nu)\Big)-a_{L}\Big(\widetilde{U}^{\prime}(\nu)\widetilde{V}(\nu)+\widetilde{U}(\nu)\widetilde{V}^{\prime}(\nu)\Big)<0,
``` |  |

since $`a_{L}>1`$, and

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
U(\nu),\widetilde{U}(\nu),V(\nu),\widetilde{V}(\nu),U^{\prime}(\nu),\widetilde{U}^{\prime}(\nu),V^{\prime}(\nu),\widetilde{V}^{\prime}(\nu)>0.
``` |  |

We first prove that $`U(\nu)<\widetilde{U}(\nu)`$. Since
$`0<2^{-\beta}<1`$, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\;<\;1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu,
``` |  |

and hence $`U(\nu)<\widetilde{U}(\nu)`$.

We second prove that $`U^{\prime}(\nu)<\widetilde{U}^{\prime}(\nu)`$. By
direct differentiation, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
U^{\prime}(\nu)=\frac{c_{\delta}}{c}\left((1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{-1/2}+\frac{c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}{2}(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{-3/2}\right).
``` |  |

Similarly,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\widetilde{U}^{\prime}(\nu)=\frac{c_{\delta}}{c}\left((2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{-1/2}+\frac{c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}{2}(2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{-3/2}\right).
``` |  |

Since
$`2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu<(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu,`$
$`U^{\prime}(\nu)<\widetilde{U}^{\prime}(\nu)`$.

We third prove that $`V(\nu)<\widetilde{V}(\nu)`$ We can rewrite
$`V(\nu)`$ as the finite geometric sum

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V(\nu)=\sum_{j=0}^{L-2}\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!j}.
``` |  |

Similarly,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\widetilde{V}(\nu)=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\cdot e^{-\beta/L}\right)^{\!j}.
``` |  |

Therefore, it is enough to show that the common ratio of the geometric
sum defining $`V(\nu)`$ is strictly smaller than the common ratio of the
geometric series defining $`\widetilde{V}(\nu)`$, namely,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}\;<\;\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\cdot e^{-\beta/L}.
``` |  |

This is equivalent to

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\;<\;e^{-2\beta/(3L)}\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu).
``` |  |

Since $`L\geq 2`$ implies $`\frac{1}{L}<\frac{3}{2}\log 2`$, we have
$`e^{\beta(\frac{3}{2}\log 2-\frac{1}{L})}>1.`$ Rearranging gives
$`e^{-2\beta/(3L)}>2^{-\beta}.`$ Moreover,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\;\leq\;2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu,
``` |  |

and

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\;\leq\;e^{-2\beta/(3L)}(1-\gamma)-e^{-2\beta/(3L)}c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu=e^{-2\beta/(3L)}(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu).
``` |  |

Combining the last two displays gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\;<\;e^{-2\beta/(3L)}(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu),
``` |  |

which proves the desired ratio inequality.

We finally prove that $`V^{\prime}(\nu)<\widetilde{V}^{\prime}(\nu)`$.
Differentiating term-by-term, we obtain

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V^{\prime}(\nu)=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\nu}\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}\right)\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{L-2}j\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!j-1},
``` |  |

and

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\widetilde{V}^{\prime}(\nu)`$ | $`\displaystyle=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\nu}\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\cdot e^{-\beta/L}\right)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}j\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\cdot e^{-\beta/L}\right)^{\!j-1}.`$ |  |

Since we have already shown that

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}\;<\;\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\cdot e^{-\beta/L},
``` |  |

it remains to prove that

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\nu}\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}\right)\;<\;\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\nu}\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\cdot e^{-\beta/L}\right).
``` |  |

For the left-hand side, direct differentiation gives

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\nu}\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}\right)`$ | $`\displaystyle=\frac{c_{\delta}}{2c}\left((1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{-3/2}+\frac{3}{2}c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{-5/2}\right)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\frac{c_{\delta}}{2c}\cdot\frac{1-\gamma+\frac{1}{2}c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}{(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{5/2}}.`$ |  |

For the right-hand side, direct differentiation yields

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\nu}\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\cdot e^{-\beta/L}\right)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\frac{c_{\delta}}{2c}\,e^{-\beta/L}\Bigg[\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{-3/2}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle-\frac{3}{2}\nu\,\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\nu}\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)\cdot\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{-5/2}\Bigg]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\frac{c_{\delta}}{2c}\,e^{-\beta/L}\cdot\frac{2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu-\frac{3}{2}\nu\,\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\nu}\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)}{\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{5/2}}.`$ |  |

Next we compute the inner derivative explicitly:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\nu}\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)`$ | $`\displaystyle=2^{-\beta}\left(-\frac{c_{\delta}}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c}\cdot\frac{c_{\delta^{\prime}}}{2}\,(a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{-3/2}\right)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}.`$ |  |

Substituting this expression back and simplifying, we obtain

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle 2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu-\frac{3}{2}\nu\,\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\nu}\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)+2^{-\beta-1}\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}+\frac{1}{2}\,c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu+3\cdot 2^{-\beta-2}\frac{c_{\delta}c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu^{2}}{c\,(a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq 2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)+\frac{1}{2}\,c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu.`$ |  |

Therefore,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\nu}\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\cdot e^{-\beta/L}\right)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{\delta}}{2c}\,e^{-\beta/L}\cdot\frac{2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)+\frac{1}{2}c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}{\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{5/2}}.`$ |  |

Moreover, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\;\leq\;2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\;\leq\;2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu),
``` |  |

Hence, plugging this into the previous display yields

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\nu}\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\cdot e^{-\beta/L}\right)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{\delta}}{2c}\cdot\frac{e^{-\beta/L}\Big(2^{3\beta/2}(1-\gamma)+2^{5\beta/2}\cdot\frac{1}{2}c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)}{(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{5/2}}.`$ |  |

Finally, since $`L\geq 2`$ implies $`\frac{1}{L}<\frac{3}{2}\log 2`$, we
have
$`e^{-\beta/L}2^{3\beta/2}=e^{\beta(\frac{3}{2}\log 2-\frac{1}{L})}>1,`$
and similarly (because $`\frac{1}{L}<\frac{5}{2}\log 2`$) we have
$`e^{-\beta/L}2^{5\beta/2}=e^{\beta(\frac{5}{2}\log 2-\frac{1}{L})}>1.`$
Therefore,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
e^{-\beta/L}\Big(2^{3\beta/2}(1-\gamma)+2^{5\beta/2}\cdot\frac{1}{2}c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)>1-\gamma+\frac{1}{2}c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu,
``` |  |

which implies

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\nu}\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\cdot e^{-\beta/L}\right)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle>\frac{c_{\delta}}{2c}\cdot\frac{1-\gamma+\frac{1}{2}c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}{(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{5/2}}=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\nu}\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}\right)`$ |  |

as desired. ∎

###### Lemma C.2.

Under the notation of
Proposition [5.5](#S5.Thmtheorem5 "Proposition 5.5. ‣ Interpreting 𝒩<0. ‣ 5.2 Main Results ‣ 5 Iterative Easy-to-Hard Curriculum for Self-Improvement ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula")
and
````
