````math
Lemma [C.1](#A3.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma C.1. ‣ C.3 Proof of Proposition ‣ C. Proofs for Section ‣ Appendix: Proofs ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula"),
for fixed $`(\beta^{\prime},\beta)`$, the threshold $`x(\nu)`$ satisfies
$`x(0)=0`$ and

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
x^{\prime}(\nu)=\frac{c_{\delta^{\prime}}}{a_{0}}+\frac{2}{a_{0}}\Big(\frac{c_{\delta}}{c(1-\gamma)}\Big)^{\!2}\nu+O\big(\nu^{5/3}\big)\quad\text{as }\nu\to 0,
``` |  |

where $`a_{0}=L/\sum_{i=1}^{L}i^{-\beta^{\prime}}`$.

###### Proof.

By the notation and proof of
Lemma [C.1](#A3.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma C.1. ‣ C.3 Proof of Proposition ‣ C. Proofs for Section ‣ Appendix: Proofs ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula"),
for fixed $`(\beta^{\prime},\beta)`$, it suffices to prove that, as
$`\nu\to 0`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
x(\nu)=\frac{c_{\delta^{\prime}}}{a_{0}}\nu+\frac{1}{a_{0}}\Big(\frac{c_{\delta}}{c(1-\gamma)}\Big)^{\!2}\nu^{2}+O(\nu^{8/3}).
``` |  |

By definition of the threshold $`x(\nu)`$, it satisfies the boundary
condition
$`\mathcal{E}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x(\nu))=-\frac{1}{2}\,(a_{L}-1)(1-\gamma).`$
Note that the right-hand side is $`\Theta(1)`$. Consequently, as
$`\nu\to 0`$, the left-hand side must also be $`\Theta(1)`$.
Equivalently, if we expand
$`\mathcal{E}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x(\nu))`$ in a Puiseux series,
then the smallest exponent of $`\nu`$ appearing in the expansion of
$`\mathcal{E}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x(\nu))`$ must be $`0`$.

We analyze $`\mathcal{E}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x(\nu))`$
term-by-term. Consider first

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
T_{1}(\nu):=\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{\,1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\,}}\cdot\frac{1-\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!L-1}}{1-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}}.
``` |  |

As $`\nu\to 0`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}=\frac{c_{\delta}}{c\sqrt{1-\gamma}}\,\nu+O(\nu^{2}).
``` |  |

Next, define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
q(\nu):=\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}.
``` |  |

Then we have

|     |                                                               |     |
|-----|---------------------------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       q(\nu)=\frac{c_{\delta}}{2c(1-\gamma)^{3/2}}\,\nu+O(\nu^{2}),
       ```                                                            |     |

Moreover,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{1-q(\nu)^{L-1}}{1-q(\nu)}=\sum_{j=0}^{L-2}q(\nu)^{j}=1+q(\nu)+O\big(q(\nu)^{2}\big)=1+O(\nu),
``` |  |

Therefore,

|     |                                                                 |     |
|-----|-----------------------------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       T_{1}(\nu)=\frac{c_{\delta}}{c\sqrt{1-\gamma}}\,\nu+O(\nu^{2}).
       ```                                                              |     |

In particular, the smallest power of $`\nu`$ appearing in the Puiseux
expansion of $`T_{1}(\nu)`$ is $`\nu`$. Consequently, $`T_{1}(\nu)`$
cannot contribute a $`\Theta(1)`$ term to
$`\mathcal{E}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x(\nu))`$ as $`\nu\to 0`$.

Next we analyze

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
T_{2}(\nu):=\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x(\nu)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!L-1}L^{-\beta}\cdot\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x(\nu)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}.
``` |  |

Our goal is to determine whether there exists a choice of $`x(\nu)`$
such that $`T_{2}(\nu)`$ can contribute a $`\Theta(1)`$ term to
$`\mathcal{E}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x(\nu))`$ as $`\nu\to 0`$. To
this end, consider the Puiseux expansion of

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
r(\nu):=\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x(\nu)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}.
``` |  |

When $`r(\nu)`$ has a negative leading exponent, $`r(\nu)\to+\infty`$ as
$`\nu\to 0`$. Then the inner radicand
$`2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-r(\nu)\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu`$ tends to
$`-\infty`$, and hence becomes negative for all sufficiently small
$`\nu`$. This violates our standing well-definedness requirement that
every denominator appearing in
$`\mathcal{E}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x(\nu))`$ remain strictly
positive.

When $`r(\nu)`$ has a positive leading exponent, $`r(\nu)=o(1)`$ as
$`\nu\to 0`$. In order for $`T_{2}(\nu)`$ to be $`\Theta(1)`$, the
factor

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\big(1-\gamma-r(\nu)\big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!L-1}
``` |  |

must contribute a negative power of $`\nu`$, which forces

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\big(1-\gamma-r(\nu)\big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\to+\infty\qquad\text{as }\nu\to 0.
``` |  |

However, this implies that in the term

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
T_{3}(\nu):=\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\cdot\frac{1}{1-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\big(1-\gamma-r(\nu)\big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\cdot e^{-\beta/L}},
``` |  |

the denominator becomes negative for all sufficiently small $`\nu`$,
again contradicting the requirement that all denominators remain
strictly positive.

Consequently, the only remaining possibility consistent with
well-definedness is that $`r(\nu)`$ has leading exponent $`0`$.
Therefore,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\big(1-\gamma-r(\nu)\big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}
``` |  |

must also have leading exponent $`0`$. We now show that this is
equivalent to the existence of a constant $`d_{2}>0`$ such that
$`r(\nu)=1-\gamma-d_{2}\,\nu^{2/3}+o(\nu^{2/3}).`$ Since the above
quantity is $`\Theta(1)`$, its denominator must satisfy
$`2^{-\beta}\big(1-\gamma-r(\nu)\big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu=\Theta(\nu^{2/3}).`$
Since $`\nu=o(\nu^{2/3})`$ as $`\nu\to 0`$, we have
$`1-\gamma-r(\nu)=\Theta(\nu^{2/3})`$. Thus there exists a constant
$`d>0`$ such that $`1-\gamma-r(\nu)=d_{2}\,\nu^{2/3}+o(\nu^{2/3}),`$
where the sign $`d>0`$ is required to ensure the radicand
$`2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma-r(\nu))-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu`$ (and hence the
denominator) remains positive. Note that the value of $`d`$ is not
universal: it is determined by the matching condition coming from the
$`\Theta(1)`$ order balance (in particular, it depends on
$`-\frac{1}{2}\,(a_{L}-1)(1-\gamma)`$). Equivalently,
$`r(\nu)=1-\gamma-d_{2}\,\nu^{2/3}+o(\nu^{2/3}),`$ as claimed.

Next, from

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x(\nu)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}=(1-\gamma)-d_{2}\,\nu^{2/3}+o(\nu^{2/3}),
``` |  |

rearranging gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\sqrt{a_{0}x(\nu)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}=\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\big((1-\gamma)-d_{2}\,\nu^{2/3}+o(\nu^{2/3})\big)}.
``` |  |

Using the expansion

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)-d_{2}\,\nu^{2/3}+o(\nu^{2/3})}=\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\left(1+\frac{d_{2}}{1-\gamma}\nu^{2/3}+o(\nu^{2/3})\right),
``` |  |

we obtain

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\sqrt{a_{0}x(\nu)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}=\frac{c_{\delta}}{c(1-\gamma)}\nu+\frac{c_{\delta}d_{2}}{c(1-\gamma)^{2}}\nu^{5/3}+o(\nu^{5/3}).
``` |  |

Squaring yields

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
a_{0}x(\nu)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu=\Big(\frac{c_{\delta}}{c(1-\gamma)}\Big)^{\!2}\nu^{2}+O(\nu^{8/3}),
``` |  |

Therefore,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
x(\nu)=\frac{c_{\delta^{\prime}}}{a_{0}}\nu+\frac{1}{a_{0}}\Big(\frac{c_{\delta}}{c(1-\gamma)}\Big)^{\!2}\nu^{2}+O(\nu^{8/3}),
``` |  |

as claimed.

Finally, we analyze the choice of $`x(\nu)`$ for which

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
T_{3}(\nu):=\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\cdot\frac{1}{1-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\big(1-\gamma-r(\nu)\big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\cdot e^{-\beta/L}}
``` |  |

contributes a $`\Theta(1)`$ term. First, as $`\nu\to 0`$ we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}=\frac{c_{\delta}}{c\sqrt{2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)}}\,\nu+O(\nu^{2}).
``` |  |

Hence, in order for $`T_{3}(\nu)`$ to be $`\Theta(1)`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
1-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\big(1-\gamma-r(\nu)\big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\cdot e^{-\beta/L}=\Theta(\nu)
``` |  |

Equivalently, there exists a constant $`d_{3}>0`$ such that

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\big(1-\gamma-r(\nu)\big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}=e^{\beta/L}\big(1-d_{3}\nu+o(\nu)\big).
``` |  |

Invert the above display to obtain

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Big(2^{-\beta}\big(1-\gamma-r(\nu)\big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}=\frac{c_{\delta}}{2c}\,e^{-\beta/L}\,\nu\cdot\frac{1}{1-d_{3}^{\prime}\nu+o(\nu)}=\frac{c_{\delta}}{2c}\,e^{-\beta/L}\,\nu\big(1+d_{3}^{\prime}\nu+o(\nu)\big),
``` |  |

i.e.,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
2^{-\beta}\big(1-\gamma-r(\nu)\big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu=\left(\frac{c_{\delta}}{2c}\right)^{\!2/3}e^{-2\beta/(3L)}\,\nu^{2/3}\Big(1+d_{3}^{\prime}\nu+o(\nu)\Big)^{2/3}.
``` |  |

Using the binomial expansion $`(1+t)^{2/3}=1+\frac{2}{3}t+o(t)`$ as
$`t\to 0`$, we obtain

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
2^{-\beta}\big(1-\gamma-r(\nu)\big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu=\left(\frac{c_{\delta}}{2c}\right)^{\!2/3}e^{-2\beta/(3L)}\,\nu^{2/3}+\frac{2}{3}\left(\frac{c_{\delta}}{2c}\right)^{\!2/3}e^{-2\beta/(3L)}\,d_{3}^{\prime}\,\nu^{5/3}+o(\nu^{5/3}).
``` |  |

Rearranging yields

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
r(\nu)=1-\gamma-2^{\beta}\left(\frac{c_{\delta}}{2c}\right)^{\!2/3}e^{-2\beta/(3L)}\,\nu^{2/3}+o(\nu^{2/3}).
``` |  |

Substituting this asymptotic form of $`r(\nu)`$ back into its definition
$`r(\nu)=\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x(\nu)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}`$
and repeating the same algebra as above yields

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
x(\nu)=\frac{c_{\delta^{\prime}}}{a_{0}}\nu+\frac{1}{a_{0}}\Big(\frac{c_{\delta}}{c(1-\gamma)}\Big)^{\!2}\nu^{2}+O(\nu^{8/3}).
``` |  |

Moreover, it is straightforward to verify that if we substitute the
$`r(\nu)`$ obtained from the $`\Theta(1)`$-balancing of $`T_{2}(\nu)`$
into $`T_{3}(\nu)`$, or conversely substitute the $`r(\nu)`$ obtained
from the $`\Theta(1)`$-balancing of $`T_{3}(\nu)`$ into $`T_{2}(\nu)`$,
then in both cases the resulting Puiseux expansion does not introduce
any negative leading power of $`\nu`$. In particular, neither
substitution violates the well-definedness regime.

Consequently, regardless of whether the $`\Theta(1)`$ constant term in
$`\mathcal{E}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x(\nu))`$ is predominantly
contributed by $`T_{2}(\nu)`$ or by $`T_{3}(\nu)`$, and regardless of
the specific constant value on the right-hand side
$`-\frac{1}{2}\,(a_{L}-1)(1-\gamma),`$ both scenarios yield the same
expansion for the threshold $`x(\nu)`$ up to order $`\nu^{2}`$:

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
x(\nu)=\frac{c_{\delta^{\prime}}}{a_{0}}\nu+\frac{1}{a_{0}}\Big(\frac{c_{\delta}}{c(1-\gamma)}\Big)^{\!2}\nu^{2}+O(\nu^{8/3}).
``` |  |

The only difference lies in higher-order coefficients (beyond the
$`\nu^{2}`$ term), which does not affect our conclusion. Which of
$`T_{2}(\nu)`$ or $`T_{3}(\nu)`$ provides the dominant $`\Theta(1)`$
contribution depends on the finer $`\Theta(1)`$ matching (i.e., the
constant-level balance) in the boundary condition
$`\mathcal{E}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x(\nu))=-\frac{1}{2}(a_{L}-1)(1-\gamma)`$,
and hence on the specific interplay among $`(\beta^{\prime},\beta,L)`$
and the constants $`(c_{\delta},c_{\delta^{\prime}},c,\gamma)`$ through
the corresponding $`\Theta(1)`$ coefficients. ∎

###### Lemma C.3.

Under the notation of
Proposition [5.5](#S5.Thmtheorem5 "Proposition 5.5. ‣ Interpreting 𝒩<0. ‣ 5.2 Main Results ‣ 5 Iterative Easy-to-Hard Curriculum for Self-Improvement ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula")
and
Lemma [C.1](#A3.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma C.1. ‣ C.3 Proof of Proposition ‣ C. Proofs for Section ‣ Appendix: Proofs ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula"),
for fixed $`(\beta^{\prime},\beta)`$, there exists a unique critical
value $`\nu_{c}>0`$ and a constant $`C(\nu_{c})>0`$ such that

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
x^{\prime}(\nu)\;=\;\frac{C(\nu_{c})}{(\nu_{c}-\nu)^{3}}\,\bigl(1+O(\nu_{c}-\nu)\bigr)\quad\text{as }\nu\uparrow\nu_{c}.
``` |  |

###### Proof.

By the notation and proof of
Lemma [C.1](#A3.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma C.1. ‣ C.3 Proof of Proposition ‣ C. Proofs for Section ‣ Appendix: Proofs ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula"),
for fixed $`(\beta^{\prime},\beta)`$, define
$`\mathcal{E}_{\infty}(\nu)\;:=\;\lim_{x_{0}\to\infty}\mathcal{E}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x_{0}).`$⁵⁵
5 The limit $`x_{0}\to\infty`$ is taken only for the auxiliary scalar
argument in the analytic extension of $`\mathcal{N}`$ and
$`\mathcal{E}`$. It does not assert that the original quantity
$`V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})`$ can exceed its admissible range. Then,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\infty}(\nu)=`$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{\,1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\,}}\cdot\frac{1-\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!L-1}}{1-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle-\;a_{L}\cdot\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{\,2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\,}}\cdot\frac{1}{1-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\big(2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\big)^{3/2}}\cdot e^{-\beta/L}}.`$ |  |

We further define
$`\mathcal{N}_{\infty}(\nu):=\lim_{x_{0}\to\infty}\mathcal{N}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x_{0}).`$

We first prove $`\mathcal{N}_{\infty}^{\prime}(\nu)>0`$ and the
existence of a unique $`\nu_{c}>0`$ such that
$`\mathcal{N}_{\infty}(\nu_{c})=0`$. It suffices to prove that
$`\mathcal{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}(\nu)<0`$ and that there exists a unique
$`\nu_{c}>0`$ such that
$`\mathcal{E}_{\infty}(\nu_{c})=-\frac{1}{2}\,(a_{L}-1)(1-\gamma).`$

In the proof of
Lemma [C.1](#A3.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma C.1. ‣ C.3 Proof of Proposition ‣ C. Proofs for Section ‣ Appendix: Proofs ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula"),
it is straightforward to verify that
$`\mathcal{E}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x_{0})`$ is monotonically
decreasing in $`\nu`$ also holds when $`x_{0}\to\infty`$, i.e., setting

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
r(\nu,x_{0})\;:=\;\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\;=\;0,
``` |  |

and the same monotonicity argument continues to hold. Hence
$`\mathcal{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}(\nu)\;<\;0.`$ Moreover, we clearly have
$`\mathcal{E}_{\infty}(0)=0`$. On the other hand, when $`\nu`$ is large
enough so that

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
1-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\big(2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\big)^{3/2}}\cdot e^{-\beta/L}
``` |  |

approaches $`0`$, the second term in $`\mathcal{E}_{\infty}(\nu)`$
diverges to $`-\infty`$, and thus
$`\mathcal{E}_{\infty}(\nu)\to-\infty.`$ Therefore, by continuity and
strict monotonicity of $`\mathcal{E}_{\infty}(\nu)`$ in $`\nu`$, there
exists a unique $`\nu_{c}>0`$ such that
$`\mathcal{E}_{\infty}(\nu_{c})=-\frac{1}{2}\,(a_{L}-1)(1-\gamma).`$

Next, define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
T(\nu,x_{0})\;:=\;1-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma-r(\nu,x_{0}))-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\cdot e^{-\beta/L},
``` |  |

and

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
T(\nu,\infty)\;=\;1-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\cdot e^{-\beta/L}.
``` |  |

Moreover, since

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma-r(\nu,x_{0}))-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu=\Big(2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)-2^{-\beta}r(\nu,x_{0}),
``` |  |

a Taylor expansion yields, as $`x_{0}\to\infty`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
T(\nu,\infty)-T(\nu,x_{0})=\frac{3c_{\delta}\nu\,2^{-\beta}e^{-\beta/L}}{4c\,\big(2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\big)^{5/2}}\,r(\nu,x_{0})\;+\;O\big(r(\nu,x_{0})^{2}\big),
``` |  |

Next, since $`T(\nu,\infty)>0`$ in the well-definedness regime, we may
expand the reciprocal around $`T(\nu,\infty)`$ as

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{1}{T(\nu,x_{0})}-\frac{1}{T(\nu,\infty)}=\frac{T(\nu,\infty)-T(\nu,x_{0})}{T(\nu,\infty)^{2}}\;+\;O\Big(\big(T(\nu,\infty)-T(\nu,x_{0})\big)^{2}\Big),\qquad x_{0}\to\infty.
``` |  |

Furthermore, since
$`T(\nu,\infty)-T(\nu,x_{0})=O\big(r(\nu,x_{0})\big)`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\left(\frac{1}{T(\nu,x_{0})}-\frac{1}{T(\nu,\infty)}\right)=C_{1}(\nu)\,r(\nu,x_{0})\;+\;O\big(r(\nu,x_{0})^{2}\big),\qquad x_{0}\to\infty,
``` |  |

where, after combining like terms, the coefficient $`C_{1}(\nu)`$ is
given by

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
C_{1}(\nu)=\frac{3\cdot 2^{-\beta}e^{-\beta/L}(c_{\delta}\nu)^{2}}{4}\,\frac{1}{c^{2}\Big(2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3}\Bigg(1-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\,e^{-\beta/L}\Bigg)^{\!2}}.
``` |  |

Second, we can similarly obtain, by a Taylor expansion around
$`r(\nu,x_{0})=0`$, that

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma-r(\nu,x_{0}))-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!L-1}L^{-\beta}\cdot r(\nu,x_{0})`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\qquad=\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!L-1}L^{-\beta}\cdot r(\nu,x_{0})\;+\;O\big(r(\nu,x_{0})^{2}\big),\qquad x_{0}\to\infty.`$ |  |

Therefore, if we define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
C_{2}(\nu):=\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!L-1}L^{-\beta},
``` |  |

then combining the previous expansion with the definition of
$`\mathcal{E}_{\infty}(\nu)`$ yields, as $`x_{0}\to\infty`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{E}_{\infty}(\nu)-\mathcal{E}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x_{0})=a_{L}\Big(C_{1}(\nu)+C_{2}(\nu)\Big)\,r(\nu,x_{0})\;+\;O\big(r(\nu,x_{0})^{2}\big).
``` |  |

Moreover, by the boundary equation defining $`x(\nu)`$,
$`\mathcal{E}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x(\nu))=-\frac{1}{2}(a_{L}-1)(1-\gamma).`$
Therefore

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{E}_{\infty}(\nu)+\frac{1}{2}(a_{L}-1)(1-\gamma)=a_{L}\Big(C_{1}(\nu)+C_{2}(\nu)\Big)\,r\big(\nu,x(\nu)\big)\;+\;O\big(r\big(\nu,x(\nu)\big)^{2}\big).
``` |  |

We next justify that $`x(\nu)`$ goes to infinity as $`\nu`$ approaches
$`\nu_{c}`$. Suppose, for contradiction, that $`x(\nu)`$ does not
diverge as $`\nu\uparrow\nu_{c}`$. Then there exist a sequence
$`\nu_{n}\uparrow\nu_{c}`$ and a constant $`M<\infty`$ such that
$`x(\nu_{n})\leq M`$ for all $`n`$. By passing to a subsequence, we may
assume $`x(\nu_{n})\to x_{\star}`$ for some $`x_{\star}\in(0,M]`$. By
continuity of $`\mathcal{E}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x_{0})`$ in
$`(\nu,x_{0})`$ within the well-definedness regime, and using the
boundary equation
$`\mathcal{E}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu_{n},x(\nu_{n}))=-\frac{1}{2}(a_{L}-1)(1-\gamma),`$
we obtain after taking $`n\to\infty`$ that

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{E}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu_{c},x_{\star})=-\frac{1}{2}(a_{L}-1)(1-\gamma).
``` |  |

However, since $`x_{\star}<\infty`$, the strict inequality above gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{E}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu_{c},x_{\star})\;<\;\mathcal{E}_{\infty}(\nu_{c})\;=\;-\frac{1}{2}(a_{L}-1)(1-\gamma),
``` |  |

a contradiction. Therefore $`x(\nu)`$ must be unbounded as
$`\nu\uparrow\nu_{c}`$. Finally, since
Lemma [C.1](#A3.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma C.1. ‣ C.3 Proof of Proposition ‣ C. Proofs for Section ‣ Appendix: Proofs ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula")
shows that $`x(\nu)`$ is monotonically increasing in $`\nu`$, the only
possibility is $`x(\nu)`$ goes to infinity as $`\nu`$ approaches
$`\nu_{c}`$ as claimed.

We next start from the expansion obtained above:

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{E}_{\infty}(\nu)+\frac{1}{2}(a_{L}-1)(1-\gamma)=a_{L}\big(C_{1}(\nu)+C_{2}(\nu)\big)\,r\big(\nu,x(\nu)\big)+R(\nu),\qquad R(\nu)=O\Big(r\big(\nu,x(\nu)\big)^{2}\Big),
``` |  |

as $`\nu\uparrow\nu_{c}`$. Obviously $`C_{1}(\nu)>0`$ and
$`C_{2}(\nu)>0`$, hence $`a_{L}\big(C_{1}(\nu)+C_{2}(\nu)\big)>0`$.
Then, by continuity, there exist $`\varepsilon>0`$ and a constant
$`M^{\prime},m>0`$ such that
$`a_{L}\big(C_{1}(\nu)+C_{2}(\nu)\big)\geq m>0`$ and
$`|R(\nu)|\leq M^{\prime}\,r\big(\nu,x(\nu)\big)^{2}`$ for all
$`\nu\in(\nu_{c}-\varepsilon,\nu_{c})`$. Since $`x(\nu)\to\infty`$ as
$`\nu\uparrow\nu_{c}`$, we have $`r\big(\nu,x(\nu)\big)\to 0`$. Hence we
may further assume that

|     |                                             |     |
|-----|---------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       M\,r\big(\nu,x(\nu)\big)\leq\frac{1}{2}\,m.
       ```                                          |     |

when $`\nu\in(\nu_{c}-\varepsilon,\nu_{c})`$. Then,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\infty}(\nu)+\frac{1}{2}(a_{L}-1)(1-\gamma)`$ | $`\displaystyle\geq a_{L}\big(C_{1}(\nu)+C_{2}(\nu)\big)\,r\big(\nu,x(\nu)\big)-|R(\nu)|`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq m\,r\big(\nu,x(\nu)\big)-M\,r\big(\nu,x(\nu)\big)^{2}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\,m\,r\big(\nu,x(\nu)\big),`$ |  |

Consequently,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
r\big(\nu,x(\nu)\big)^{2}=O\left(\left(\mathcal{E}_{\infty}(\nu)+\frac{1}{2}(a_{L}-1)(1-\gamma)\right)^{\!2}\right).
``` |  |

Now,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
r\big(\nu,x(\nu)\big)=\frac{\mathcal{E}_{\infty}(\nu)+\frac{1}{2}(a_{L}-1)(1-\gamma)}{a_{L}\big(C_{1}(\nu)+C_{2}(\nu)\big)}-\frac{R(\nu)}{a_{L}\big(C_{1}(\nu)+C_{2}(\nu)\big)}.
``` |  |

Therefore

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{R(\nu)}{a_{L}\big(C_{1}(\nu)+C_{2}(\nu)\big)}=O\Big(r\big(\nu,x(\nu)\big)^{2}\Big)=O\left(\left(\mathcal{E}_{\infty}(\nu)+\frac{1}{2}(a_{L}-1)(1-\gamma)\right)^{\!2}\right),
``` |  |

and

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
r\big(\nu,x(\nu)\big)=\frac{\mathcal{E}_{\infty}(\nu)+\frac{1}{2}(a_{L}-1)(1-\gamma)}{a_{L}\big(C_{1}(\nu)+C_{2}(\nu)\big)}+O\left(\left(\mathcal{E}_{\infty}(\nu)+\frac{1}{2}(a_{L}-1)(1-\gamma)\right)^{\!2}\right),\qquad\nu\uparrow\nu_{c}.
``` |  |

By differentiability of $`\mathcal{E}_{\infty}`$ at $`\nu_{c}`$ and the
identity
$`\mathcal{E}_{\infty}(\nu_{c})=-\frac{1}{2}(a_{L}-1)(1-\gamma)`$, we
have the first-order expansion

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{E}_{\infty}(\nu)+\frac{1}{2}(a_{L}-1)(1-\gamma)=\mathcal{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}(\nu_{c})\,(\nu-\nu_{c})+o(\nu-\nu_{c})=\bigl(-\mathcal{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}(\nu_{c})\bigr)\,(\nu_{c}-\nu)+o(\nu_{c}-\nu),\qquad\nu\uparrow\nu_{c},
``` |  |

where $`-\mathcal{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}(\nu_{c})>0`$. Hence, we obtain

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
r\big(\nu,x(\nu)\big)=\frac{-\mathcal{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}(\nu_{c})}{a_{L}\big(C_{1}(\nu)+C_{2}(\nu)\big)}\,(\nu_{c}-\nu)\;+\;O\big((\nu_{c}-\nu)^{2}\big)=\frac{-\mathcal{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}(\nu_{c})(\nu_{c}-\nu)}{a_{L}\big(C_{1}(\nu)+C_{2}(\nu)\big)}\,\Big(1+O(\nu_{c}-\nu)\Big).
``` |  |

Now invert the definition

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
r\big(\nu,x(\nu)\big)=\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x(\nu)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}
``` |  |

to get the identity

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
a_{0}x(\nu)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu=\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\,r\big(\nu,x(\nu)\big)}\right)^{\!2}.
``` |  |

Since

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\,r\big(\nu,x(\nu)\big)}\right)^{\!2}=\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu\,a_{L}\big(C_{1}(\nu)+C_{2}(\nu)\big)}{c\,\bigl(-\mathcal{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}(\nu_{c})\bigr)}\right)^{\!2}\frac{1}{(\nu_{c}-\nu)^{2}}\Big(1+O(\nu_{c}-\nu)\Big),\qquad\nu\uparrow\nu_{c},
``` |  |

we have,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
x(\nu)=\frac{c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}{a_{0}}+\frac{1}{a_{0}}\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu\,a_{L}\big(C_{1}(\nu)+C_{2}(\nu)\big)}{c\,\bigl(-\mathcal{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}(\nu_{c})\bigr)}\right)^{\!2}\frac{1}{(\nu_{c}-\nu)^{2}}\Big(1+O(\nu_{c}-\nu)\Big).
``` |  |

Finally, since $`\nu=\nu_{c}+O(\nu_{c}-\nu)`$ and
$`C_{1}(\nu)+C_{2}(\nu)=C_{1}(\nu_{c})+C_{2}(\nu_{c})+O(\nu_{c}-\nu)`$
by continuity, we may replace $`\nu`$ by $`\nu_{c}`$ and
$`C_{1}(\nu)+C_{2}(\nu)`$ by $`C_{1}(\nu_{c})+C_{2}(\nu_{c})`$ inside
the prefactor at the cost of a multiplicative $`(1+O(\nu_{c}-\nu))`$
factor, giving

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
x(\nu)=\frac{1}{a_{0}}\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu_{c}\,a_{L}\big(C_{1}(\nu_{c})+C_{2}(\nu_{c})\big)}{c\,\bigl(-\mathcal{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}(\nu_{c})\bigr)}\right)^{\!2}\frac{1}{(\nu_{c}-\nu)^{2}}\Big(1+O(\nu_{c}-\nu)\Big).
``` |  |

Differentiating the asymptotic expansion gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
x^{\prime}(\nu)=\frac{C(\nu_{c})}{(\nu_{c}-\nu)^{3}}\Big(1+O(\nu_{c}-\nu)\Big),
``` |  |

where

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
C(\nu_{c})=\frac{2}{a_{0}}\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu_{c}\,a_{L}\big(C_{1}(\nu_{c})+C_{2}(\nu_{c})\big)}{c\,\bigl(-\mathcal{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}(\nu_{c})\bigr)}\right)^{\!2}.
``` |  |

This completes the proof. ∎

#### C.4  Proof of Corollary [5.6](#S5.Thmtheorem6 "Corollary 5.6 (Informal version; formal version stated in Corollary ). ‣ Interpreting 𝒩<0. ‣ 5.2 Main Results ‣ 5 Iterative Easy-to-Hard Curriculum for Self-Improvement ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula")

###### Corollary C.4 (Formal version of Corollary [5.6](#S5.Thmtheorem6 "Corollary 5.6 (Informal version; formal version stated in Corollary ). ‣ Interpreting 𝒩<0. ‣ 5.2 Main Results ‣ 5 Iterative Easy-to-Hard Curriculum for Self-Improvement ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula")).

In
Proposition [5.5](#S5.Thmtheorem5 "Proposition 5.5. ‣ Interpreting 𝒩<0. ‣ 5.2 Main Results ‣ 5 Iterative Easy-to-Hard Curriculum for Self-Improvement ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula"),
fix any initialization $`V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})\in(0,1-\gamma)`$,
and let $`\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta)`$ be defined by the
threshold equation
$`x\big(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta)\big)=V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0}).`$
Then

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta)\;=\;\sup\big\{\,\nu>0:\ \mathcal{N}\big(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})\big)<0\,\big\}.
``` |  |

Moreover, (i) fixing $`\beta^{\prime}`$, for $`\beta>\beta^{\prime}`$,
$`\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta)`$ is decreasing in $`\beta`$; (ii)
fixing $`\beta`$, for $`\beta^{\prime}\in(0,\beta)`$,
$`\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta)`$ is increasing in
$`\beta^{\prime}`$; (iii) fixing $`\Delta=\beta-\beta^{\prime}`$ and
writing $`\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta)`$ as
$`\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)`$, when
$`\beta^{\prime}`$ is small,
$`\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)`$ is increasing in
$`\beta^{\prime}`$ and

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)=\frac{c(1-\gamma)^{3/2}\log\!\Big(\frac{L}{(L!)^{1/L}}\Big)}{2c_{\delta}\big(2^{\Delta/2}-1\big)}\,\beta^{\prime}\;+\;o(\beta^{\prime})
``` |  |

as $`\beta^{\prime}\to 0`$. Moreover, let $`\nu_{T}>0`$ be the (unique)
solution to

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{\,1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\,}}\cdot\frac{1-\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!L-1}}{1-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}}=\frac{1-\gamma}{2}.
``` |  |

Define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
h(\beta^{\prime}):=\frac{a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})-1\big)}{a_{L}^{\prime}(\beta^{\prime})}.
``` |  |

Then for any fixed $`B>h^{-1}\!\left(\frac{2}{\log 2}\right)>0,`$ there
exists a constant $`\nu_{0}(B)>0`$ such that whenever
$`\nu_{T}<\nu_{0}(B)`$, we have
$`\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)<\nu_{T}`$ for all
$`\beta^{\prime}\in(0,B]`$, and
$`\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)`$ is guaranteed to
be first increasing and then decreasing in $`\beta^{\prime}`$ on
$`[0,B]`$, with a unique optimizer on this interval. Moreover, for
sufficiently large $`\beta^{\prime}`$, the threshold
$`\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)`$ satisfies
$`\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)=O(2^{-\beta^{\prime}})`$
and hence $`\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)\to 0`$ as
$`\beta^{\prime}\to\infty.`$

###### Proof.

By
Proposition [5.5](#S5.Thmtheorem5 "Proposition 5.5. ‣ Interpreting 𝒩<0. ‣ 5.2 Main Results ‣ 5 Iterative Easy-to-Hard Curriculum for Self-Improvement ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula"),
for fixed $`(\beta^{\prime},\beta)`$ we have
$`\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{N}}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu)=\bigl(x(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu),\,1-\gamma\bigr)`$
and $`x(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu)`$ is monotonically increasing in
$`\nu`$. Therefore, for any $`0<\nu<\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta)`$
we have $`x(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu)<V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})`$, so
$`V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{N}}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu)`$,
i.e.,
$`\mathcal{N}\big(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})\big)<0.`$
Conversely, for any $`\nu\geq\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta)`$ we have
$`x(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu)\geq V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})`$, so
$`V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})\notin\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{N}}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu)`$,
i.e.,
$`\mathcal{N}\big(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})\big)\geq 0.`$
Hence,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta)\;=\;\sup\Big\{\,\nu>0:\ \mathcal{N}\big(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})\big)<0\,\Big\}.
``` |  |

The remaining claims follow by directly combining
Lemma [C.5](#A3.Thmtheorem5 "Lemma C.5. ‣ C.4 Proof of Corollary ‣ C. Proofs for Section ‣ Appendix: Proofs ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula"),
Lemma [C.6](#A3.Thmtheorem6 "Lemma C.6. ‣ C.4 Proof of Corollary ‣ C. Proofs for Section ‣ Appendix: Proofs ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula"),
Lemma [C.7](#A3.Thmtheorem7 "Lemma C.7. ‣ C.4 Proof of Corollary ‣ C. Proofs for Section ‣ Appendix: Proofs ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula"),
and
Lemma [C.8](#A3.Thmtheorem8 "Lemma C.8. ‣ C.4 Proof of Corollary ‣ C. Proofs for Section ‣ Appendix: Proofs ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula").
∎

###### Lemma C.5.

In
Proposition [5.5](#S5.Thmtheorem5 "Proposition 5.5. ‣ Interpreting 𝒩<0. ‣ 5.2 Main Results ‣ 5 Iterative Easy-to-Hard Curriculum for Self-Improvement ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula"),
fix any initialization $`V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})\in(0,1-\gamma)`$,
and define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta)\;=\;\sup\Big\{\,\nu>0:\ \mathcal{N}\big(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})\big)<0\,\Big\}.
``` |  |

Then, fixing $`\beta^{\prime}`$, for $`\beta>\beta^{\prime}`$,
$`\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta)`$ is decreasing in $`\beta`$.

###### Proof.

Let $`x_{0}:=V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})`$ for convenience. By
Theorem [5.2](#S5.Thmtheorem2 "Theorem 5.2. ‣ 5.2 Main Results ‣ 5 Iterative Easy-to-Hard Curriculum for Self-Improvement ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula"),
the inequality $`\mathcal{N}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x_{0})<0`$ is
equivalent to

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{E}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x_{0})>-\frac{1}{2}\,(a_{L}-1)(1-\gamma).
``` |  |

Note that when $`\beta^{\prime}>0`$ is fixed, the right-hand side above
is a fixed constant. Moreover,
Lemma [C.1](#A3.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma C.1. ‣ C.3 Proof of Proposition ‣ C. Proofs for Section ‣ Appendix: Proofs ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula")
has already shown that $`\mathcal{E}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x_{0})`$
is monotonically (strictly) decreasing in $`\nu`$. Therefore, to prove
that $`\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta)`$ is monotonically decreasing
in $`\beta`$, it suffices to show that for each fixed
$`(\beta^{\prime},\nu,x_{0})`$ in the well-definedness regime,
$`\mathcal{E}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x_{0})`$ is strictly decreasing
as a function of $`\beta`$ (with $`\beta>\beta^{\prime}`$). Indeed,
define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Phi(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu)\;:=\;\mathcal{E}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x_{0})+\frac{1}{2}\,(a_{L}-1)(1-\gamma).
``` |  |

Then
$`\Phi\big(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta)\big)=0`$.
If $`\mathcal{E}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x_{0})`$ is strictly
decreasing in $`\beta`$, equivalently
$`\partial_{\beta}\Phi(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu)<0`$, since
$`\partial_{\nu}\Phi(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu)<0`$ and
$`\partial_{\nu}\Phi\big(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta)\big)\neq 0`$,
the implicit function theorem applies to the equation
$`\Phi(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu)=0`$ around
$`\nu=\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta)`$ and yields

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{\partial}{\partial\beta}\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta)\;=\;-\frac{\partial_{\beta}\Phi\big(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta)\big)}{\partial_{\nu}\Phi\big(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta)\big)}\;<\;0.
``` |  |

Therefore, it suffices to prove that

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\cdot\frac{1}{1-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\cdot e^{-\beta/L}}
``` |  |

and

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!L-1}L^{-\beta}\cdot\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}
``` |  |

are both strictly increasing as a function of $`\beta`$. For

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\widetilde{U}(\beta)=\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}.
``` |  |

Clearly, as $`\beta`$ increases, $`2^{-\beta}`$ decreases, and hence
$`\widetilde{U}(\beta)`$ is strictly increasing in $`\beta`$. Next
consider

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\widetilde{V}(\beta)=\frac{1}{1-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\cdot e^{-\beta/L}}.
``` |  |

Differentiating gives

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\quad\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\beta}\log\!\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\cdot e^{-\beta/L}\right)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=-\frac{3}{2}\cdot\frac{-\log 2\cdot 2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)}{2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}-\frac{1}{L}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\frac{3}{2}\log 2\cdot\frac{2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)}{2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}-\frac{1}{L}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle>\frac{3}{2}\log 2-\frac{1}{L}`$ |  |

Since $`L\geq 2`$, we have $`\frac{3}{2}\log 2-\frac{1}{L}>0`$, and
hence the above derivative is strictly positive. Thus

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\cdot e^{-\beta/L}
``` |  |

is strictly increasing in $`\beta`$, which implies that
$`\widetilde{V}(\beta)`$ is strictly increasing in $`\beta`$. Combining
with the monotonicity of $`\widetilde{U}(\beta)`$, we conclude that
$`\widetilde{U}(\beta)\widetilde{V}(\beta)`$ is strictly increasing in
$`\beta`$.

Next, consider

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
T_{2}(\beta)=\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!L-1}L^{-\beta}\cdot\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}.
``` |  |

We compute the derivative of $`\log T_{2}(\beta)`$. Since the factor
$`\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}`$ does
not depend on $`\beta`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\beta}\log T_{2}(\beta)`$ | $`\displaystyle=(L-1)\cdot\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\beta}\log\!\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\right)-\log L`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=(L-1)\cdot\frac{3}{2}\log 2\cdot\frac{2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)}{2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}-\log L`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle>(L-1)\cdot\frac{3}{2}\log 2-\log L.`$ |  |

In particular, since $`L\geq 2`$, we have
$`(L-1)\cdot\frac{3}{2}\log 2-\log L>0`$, and therefore $`T_{2}(\beta)`$
is strictly increasing in $`\beta`$. Consequently, the
$`\beta`$-dependent terms inside the bracket in
$`\mathcal{E}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x_{0})`$ are strictly increasing
in $`\beta`$, and since the bracket is multiplied by the negative
coefficient $`-a_{L}`$, it follows that
$`\mathcal{E}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x_{0})`$ is strictly decreasing
in $`\beta`$. This completes the proof. ∎

###### Lemma C.6.

In
Proposition [5.5](#S5.Thmtheorem5 "Proposition 5.5. ‣ Interpreting 𝒩<0. ‣ 5.2 Main Results ‣ 5 Iterative Easy-to-Hard Curriculum for Self-Improvement ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula"),
fix any initialization $`V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})\in(0,1-\gamma)`$,
and define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta)\;=\;\sup\Big\{\,\nu>0:\ \mathcal{N}\big(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})\big)<0\,\Big\}.
``` |  |

Then, fixing $`\beta`$, for $`\beta^{\prime}\in(0,\beta)`$,
$`\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta)`$ is increasing in
$`\beta^{\prime}`$.

###### Proof.

Let $`x_{0}:=V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})`$ for convenience. Define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
T_{1}(\nu):=\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{\,1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\,}}\cdot\frac{1-\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!L-1}}{1-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}},
``` |  |

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
T_{2}(\nu,\beta^{\prime}):=\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}(\beta^{\prime})x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!L-1}L^{-\beta}\cdot\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}(\beta^{\prime})x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}},
``` |  |

and

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
T_{3}(\nu,\beta^{\prime}):=\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\cdot\frac{1}{1-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}(\beta^{\prime})x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\cdot e^{-\beta/L}}.
``` |  |

Then $`\mathcal{N}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x_{0})<0`$ is equivalent to

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
T_{1}(\nu)-a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\big(T_{2}(\nu,\beta^{\prime})+T_{3}(\nu,\beta^{\prime})\big)>-\frac{1}{2}\,(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})-1)(1-\gamma).
``` |  |

Equivalently,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
T_{1}(\nu)-\frac{1-\gamma}{2}+a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\left(\frac{1-\gamma}{2}-T_{2}(\nu,\beta^{\prime})-T_{3}(\nu,\beta^{\prime})\right)>0.
``` |  |

We first verify that both $`a_{0}(\beta^{\prime})`$ and
$`a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})`$ are strictly increasing in $`\beta^{\prime}`$.
Recall the definitions

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
a_{0}(\beta^{\prime})\;=\;\frac{L}{\sum_{i=1}^{L}i^{-\beta^{\prime}}},\qquad a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\;=\;\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{L}i^{-\beta^{\prime}}}{L^{1-\beta^{\prime}}}.
``` |  |

$`a_{0}(\beta^{\prime})`$ is strictly increasing in $`\beta^{\prime}`$
since

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\beta^{\prime}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{L}i^{-\beta^{\prime}}\right)<0,
``` |  |

Next,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})=\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{L}\left(\frac{L}{i}\right)^{\beta^{\prime}}.
``` |  |

Therefore $`a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})`$ is strictly increasing in
$`\beta^{\prime}`$. Then, we conclude that both
$`T_{2}(\nu,\beta^{\prime})`$ and $`T_{3}(\nu,\beta^{\prime})`$ are
strictly decreasing in $`\beta^{\prime}`$.

Suppose that for every $`\beta^{\prime}\in(0,\beta)`$, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{1-\gamma}{2}-T_{2}\big(\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta),\beta^{\prime}\big)-T_{3}\big(\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta),\beta^{\prime}\big)\geq 0.
``` |  |

Then for any $`0<\beta^{\prime}_{1}<\beta^{\prime}_{2}<\beta`$, using
that $`a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})`$ is strictly increasing in
$`\beta^{\prime}`$ and that $`T_{2}(\nu,\beta^{\prime})`$ and
$`T_{3}(\nu,\beta^{\prime})`$ are strictly decreasing in
$`\beta^{\prime}`$, we obtain

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle T_{1}\big(\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime}_{1},\beta)\big)-\frac{1-\gamma}{2}+a_{L}(\beta^{\prime}_{2})\left(\frac{1-\gamma}{2}-T_{2}\big((\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime}_{1},\beta),\beta^{\prime}_{2}\big)-T_{3}\big((\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime}_{1},\beta),\beta^{\prime}_{2}\big)\right)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\qquad>T_{1}\big(\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime}_{1},\beta)\big)-\frac{1-\gamma}{2}+a_{L}(\beta^{\prime}_{1})\left(\frac{1-\gamma}{2}-T_{2}\big((\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime}_{1},\beta),\beta^{\prime}_{1}\big)-T_{3}\big((\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime}_{1},\beta),\beta^{\prime}_{1}\big)\right)=0.`$ |  |

Since
Lemma [C.1](#A3.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma C.1. ‣ C.3 Proof of Proposition ‣ C. Proofs for Section ‣ Appendix: Proofs ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula")
shows that $`\mathcal{E}(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,x_{0})`$ is strictly
decreasing in $`\nu`$, it follows that
$`\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime}_{2},\beta)>\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime}_{1},\beta).`$
Therefore, it remains to prove that for every
$`\beta^{\prime}\in(0,\beta)`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{1-\gamma}{2}-T_{2}\big(\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta),\beta^{\prime}\big)-T_{3}\big(\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta),\beta^{\prime}\big)\geq 0.
``` |  |

Note that

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
T_{1}\big(\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta)\big)-\frac{1-\gamma}{2}+a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\left(\frac{1-\gamma}{2}-T_{2}\big(\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta),\beta^{\prime}\big)-T_{3}\big(\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta),\beta^{\prime}\big)\right)=0.
``` |  |

Since $`a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})>0`$, it suffices to prove that
$`T_{1}\big(\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta)\big)-\frac{1-\gamma}{2}<0.`$

We next prove that $`T_{1}(\nu)`$ is strictly increasing in $`\nu`$
within the well-definedness regime. This is because
$`\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{\,1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\,}}`$ is
positive and clearly strictly increasing in $`\nu`$. Moreover,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{1-\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!L-1}}{1-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}}=\sum_{j=0}^{L-2}\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!j},
``` |  |

where
$`\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}`$ is
positive and clearly strictly increasing in $`\nu`$. Therefore, let
$`\nu_{T}`$ denote a (necessarily unique, by strict monotonicity) value
satisfying $`T_{1}(\nu_{T})-\frac{1-\gamma}{2}=0.`$ Then, since
$`T_{1}(\nu)`$ is increasing in $`\nu`$, it suffices to prove that
$`0<\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta)<\nu_{T}.`$

By the proof of
Lemma [C.1](#A3.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma C.1. ‣ C.3 Proof of Proposition ‣ C. Proofs for Section ‣ Appendix: Proofs ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula"),
we have $`T_{3}(\nu,\beta^{\prime})>T_{1}(\nu)`$ . Hence
$`T_{3}(\nu,\beta^{\prime})+T_{2}(\nu,\beta^{\prime})>T_{1}(\nu)`$, and
therefore,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle T_{1}\big(\nu_{T}\big)-\frac{1-\gamma}{2}+a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\left(\frac{1-\gamma}{2}-T_{2}\big(\nu_{T},\beta^{\prime}\big)-T_{3}\big(\nu_{T},\beta^{\prime}\big)\right)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\left(\frac{1-\gamma}{2}-T_{2}\big(\nu_{T},\beta^{\prime}\big)-T_{3}\big(\nu_{T},\beta^{\prime}\big)\right)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle<a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\left(\frac{1-\gamma}{2}-T_{1}(\nu_{T})\right)=0.`$ |  |

On the other hand, since
$`T_{1}(0)=T_{2}(0,\beta^{\prime})=T_{3}(0,\beta^{\prime})=0`$, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
T_{1}(0)-\frac{1-\gamma}{2}+a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\left(\frac{1-\gamma}{2}-T_{2}(0,\beta^{\prime})-T_{3}(0,\beta^{\prime})\right)=\big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})-1\big)\frac{1-\gamma}{2}>0.
``` |  |

Finally, since

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
T_{1}(\nu)-\frac{1-\gamma}{2}+a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\left(\frac{1-\gamma}{2}-T_{2}(\nu,\beta^{\prime})-T_{3}(\nu,\beta^{\prime})\right)
``` |  |

is strictly decreasing in $`\nu`$ as well (for each fixed
$`\beta^{\prime}`$), we must have
$`0<\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta)<\nu_{T}`$, as desired. ∎

###### Lemma C.7.

In
Proposition [5.5](#S5.Thmtheorem5 "Proposition 5.5. ‣ Interpreting 𝒩<0. ‣ 5.2 Main Results ‣ 5 Iterative Easy-to-Hard Curriculum for Self-Improvement ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula"),
fix any initialization $`V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})\in(0,1-\gamma)`$,
and define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta)\;=\;\sup\Big\{\,\nu>0:\ \mathcal{N}\big(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})\big)<0\,\Big\}.
``` |  |

Fix $`\Delta=\beta-\beta^{\prime}>0`$ and write
$`\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta)=\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)`$.
When $`\beta^{\prime}`$ is small,
$`\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)`$ is increasing in
$`\beta^{\prime}`$ and

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)=\frac{c(1-\gamma)^{3/2}\log\!\Big(\frac{L}{(L!)^{1/L}}\Big)}{2c_{\delta}\big(2^{\Delta/2}-1\big)}\,\beta^{\prime}\;+\;o(\beta^{\prime})\qquad\text{as }\beta^{\prime}\to 0.
``` |  |

###### Proof.

Let $`x_{0}:=V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})`$ and
$`\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime}):=\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)`$
convenience. Following the notation of
Theorem [5.2](#S5.Thmtheorem2 "Theorem 5.2. ‣ 5.2 Main Results ‣ 5 Iterative Easy-to-Hard Curriculum for Self-Improvement ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula"),
we write

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{N}(\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}+\Delta,\nu,x_{0})=-\mathcal{E}(\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}+\Delta,\nu,x_{0})-\frac{1}{2}\,\big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})-1\big)(1-\gamma).
``` |  |

Moreover,
$`\mathcal{N}(\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}+\Delta,\nu,x_{0})`$ is
increasing in $`\nu`$, and for every $`\beta^{\prime}>0`$ we have
$`\mathcal{N}(\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}+\Delta,0,x_{0})<0.`$
Furthermore, $`\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})`$ satisfies

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{N}\big(\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}+\Delta,\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime}),x_{0}\big)=0,
``` |  |

and note that when $`\beta^{\prime}=0`$, the corresponding threshold
satisfies $`\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(0)=0.`$

We first compute the partial derivatives of
$`\mathcal{N}(\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}+\Delta,\nu,x_{0})`$ with
respect to $`\nu`$ and $`\beta^{\prime}`$ at
$`(\beta^{\prime},\nu)=(0,0)`$, namely
$`\partial_{\nu}\mathcal{N}(0,\Delta,0,x_{0})`$ and
$`\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}\mathcal{N}(0,\Delta,0,x_{0})`$. For
$`\partial_{\nu}\mathcal{N}(0,\Delta,0,x_{0})=-\partial_{\nu}\mathcal{E}(0,\Delta,0,x_{0})`$,
note that the derivative of
$`\mathcal{E}(\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}+\Delta,\nu,x_{0})`$ at
$`\nu=0`$ only depends on its linear term in $`\nu`$. Since

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{\,1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\,}}\cdot\frac{1-\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!L-1}}{1-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}}=\frac{c_{\delta}}{c\sqrt{\,1-\gamma\,}}\,\nu+o(\nu),
``` |  |

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{2^{-\beta}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\cdot\frac{1}{1-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\cdot e^{-\beta/L}}=2^{\Delta/2}\frac{c_{\delta}}{c\sqrt{\,1-\gamma\,}}\,\nu+o(\nu),
``` |  |

and

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\Big(2^{-\beta}\Big(1-\gamma-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}\Big)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\Big)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!L-1}L^{-\beta}\cdot\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{a_{0}x_{0}-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu}}=O(\nu^{L}),
``` |  |

we obtain

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\partial_{\nu}\mathcal{N}(0,\Delta,0,x_{0})=-\partial_{\nu}\mathcal{E}(0,\Delta,0,x_{0})=\Big(2^{\Delta/2}-1\Big)\frac{c_{\delta}}{c\sqrt{\,1-\gamma\,}}>0.
``` |  |

Next we compute
$`\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}\mathcal{N}(0,\Delta,0,x_{0})`$. Since
$`\mathcal{E}(\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}+\Delta,\nu,x_{0})\equiv 0`$
at $`\nu=0`$, we have
$`\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}\mathcal{N}(0,\Delta,0,x_{0})=-\frac{1}{2}\,a_{L}^{\prime}(0)(1-\gamma).`$
Recall that
$`a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})=\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{L}i^{-\beta^{\prime}}}{L^{1-\beta^{\prime}}}.`$
Hence

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\log a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})=\log\!\left(\sum_{i=1}^{L}i^{-\beta^{\prime}}\right)-\log L+\beta^{\prime}\log L,
``` |  |

and

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left.\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\beta^{\prime}}\log a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\right|_{\beta^{\prime}=0}=\frac{-\sum_{i=1}^{L}\log i}{L}+\log L=\log\!\Big(\frac{L}{(L!)^{1/L}}\Big).
``` |  |

Since $`a_{L}(0)=\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{L}1}{L}=1`$, it follows that

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
a_{L}^{\prime}(0)=a_{L}(0)\cdot\left.\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\beta^{\prime}}\log a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\right|_{\beta^{\prime}=0}=\log\!\Big(\frac{L}{(L!)^{1/L}}\Big)>0.
``` |  |

Therefore,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}\mathcal{N}(0,\Delta,0,x_{0})=-\frac{1}{2}\,a_{L}^{\prime}(0)(1-\gamma)=-\frac{1}{2}\,\log\!\Big(\frac{L}{(L!)^{1/L}}\Big)\,(1-\gamma)<0.
``` |  |

Finally, since $`\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})`$ is defined
implicitly by
$`\mathcal{N}\big(\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}+\Delta,\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime}),x_{0}\big)=0,`$
the implicit function theorem yields

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\beta^{\prime}}\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(0)=-\frac{\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}\mathcal{N}(0,\Delta,0,x_{0})}{\partial_{\nu}\mathcal{N}(0,\Delta,0,x_{0})}=\frac{c(1-\gamma)^{3/2}\log\!\Big(\frac{L}{(L!)^{1/L}}\Big)}{2c_{\delta}\big(2^{\Delta/2}-1\big)}\;>\;0.
``` |  |

Therefore, as $`\beta^{\prime}\to 0`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})=\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(0)+\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\beta^{\prime}}\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(0)\,\beta^{\prime}+o(\beta^{\prime})=\frac{c(1-\gamma)^{3/2}\log\!\Big(\frac{L}{(L!)^{1/L}}\Big)}{2c_{\delta}\big(2^{\Delta/2}-1\big)}\,\beta^{\prime}\;+\;o(\beta^{\prime}).
``` |  |

This completes the proof. ∎

###### Lemma C.8.

In
Proposition [5.5](#S5.Thmtheorem5 "Proposition 5.5. ‣ Interpreting 𝒩<0. ‣ 5.2 Main Results ‣ 5 Iterative Easy-to-Hard Curriculum for Self-Improvement ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula"),
fix any initialization $`V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})\in(0,1-\gamma)`$,
and define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta)\;=\;\sup\Big\{\,\nu>0:\ \mathcal{N}\big(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\nu,V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})\big)<0\,\Big\}.
``` |  |

Fix $`\Delta=\beta-\beta^{\prime}>0`$ and write
$`\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta)=\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)`$.
Let $`\nu_{T}>0`$ be the solution to

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{c\sqrt{\,1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu\,}}\cdot\frac{1-\left(\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}\right)^{\!L-1}}{1-\frac{c_{\delta}\nu}{2c\,(1-\gamma-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\nu)^{3/2}}}=\frac{1-\gamma}{2}.
``` |  |

Define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
h(\beta^{\prime}):=\frac{a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})-1\big)}{a_{L}^{\prime}(\beta^{\prime})}.
``` |  |

Then for any fixed $`B>h^{-1}\!\left(\frac{2}{\log 2}\right)>0,`$ there
exists a constant $`\nu_{0}(B)>0`$ such that whenever
$`\nu_{T}<\nu_{0}(B)`$, we have
$`\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)<\nu_{T}`$ for all
$`\beta^{\prime}\in(0,B]`$, and
$`\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)`$ is guaranteed to
be first increasing and then decreasing in $`\beta^{\prime}`$ on
$`[0,B]`$, with a unique optimizer on this interval. Moreover, for
sufficiently large $`\beta^{\prime}`$, the threshold
$`\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)`$ satisfies
$`\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)=O(2^{-\beta^{\prime}})`$
and hence $`\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)\to 0`$ as
$`\beta^{\prime}\to\infty.`$

###### Proof.

Let $`x_{0}:=V_{p_{0}}(\hat{\theta}_{0})`$,
$`\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime}):=\nu^{\star}(\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)`$,
and write
$`\mathcal{N}(\beta^{\prime},\nu):=\mathcal{N}(\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}+\Delta,\nu,x_{0})`$
for brevity.

First, by the proof of
Lemma [C.6](#A3.Thmtheorem6 "Lemma C.6. ‣ C.4 Proof of Corollary ‣ C. Proofs for Section ‣ Appendix: Proofs ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula"),
we directly obtain
$`0<\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})<\nu_{T}<\nu_{0}(B),`$ where
$`\nu_{0}(B)`$ will be chosen later. On any interval
$`\beta^{\prime}\in[0,B]`$, when $`\nu_{T}`$ is sufficiently small, all
radicands appearing in $`\mathcal{N}`$ remain strictly positive. Thus,
for $`\beta^{\prime}\in[0,B]`$ and $`0\leq\nu\leq\nu_{T}`$, we have the
expansion

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{N}(\beta^{\prime},\nu)=-\frac{1}{2}\,\big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})-1\big)(1-\gamma)+\frac{c_{\delta}}{c\sqrt{1-\gamma}}\Big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\,2^{(\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)/2}-1\Big)\nu+R_{B}(\beta^{\prime},\nu),
``` |  |

where there exist constants $`C_{0}(B),C_{1}(B),C_{2}(B)>0`$ such that

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
|R_{B}(\beta^{\prime},\nu)|\leq C_{0}(B)\nu^{2},\qquad\big|\partial_{\nu}R_{B}(\beta^{\prime},\nu)\big|\leq C_{1}(B)\nu,\qquad\big|\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}R_{B}(\beta^{\prime},\nu)\big|\leq C_{2}(B)\nu^{2}.
``` |  |

Moreover, note that for fixed $`\Delta>0`$, we always have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{c_{\delta}}{c\sqrt{1-\gamma}}\Big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\,2^{(\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)/2}-1\Big)>0,
``` |  |

and on $`[0,B]`$ it is bounded away from zero.

Define the linear approximation of $`\mathcal{N}(\beta^{\prime},\nu)`$
by dropping the remainder term:

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{N}_{\ell}(\beta^{\prime},\nu)=-\frac{1}{2}\,\big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})-1\big)(1-\gamma)+\frac{c_{\delta}}{c\sqrt{1-\gamma}}\Big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\,2^{(\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)/2}-1\Big)\,\nu.
``` |  |

Then the linearized root is given by

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\nu^{\ell}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime}):=\frac{c(1-\gamma)^{3/2}\big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})-1\big)}{2c_{\delta}\Big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\,2^{(\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)/2}-1\Big)}.
``` |  |

Note that the behavior of $`\nu^{\ell}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})`$ as
$`\beta^{\prime}\to 0`$ is consistent with
Lemma [C.7](#A3.Thmtheorem7 "Lemma C.7. ‣ C.4 Proof of Corollary ‣ C. Proofs for Section ‣ Appendix: Proofs ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula").
We now verify this. Recall that
$`a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})=\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{L}i^{-\beta^{\prime}}}{L^{1-\beta^{\prime}}}.`$
With explicit expansions we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})=1+\log\!\Big(\frac{L}{(L!)^{1/L}}\Big)\,\beta^{\prime}+o(\beta^{\prime}).
``` |  |

Next, since

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
2^{(\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)/2}=2^{\Delta/2}\Big(1+\frac{\log 2}{2}\beta^{\prime}+o(\beta^{\prime})\Big),
``` |  |

we obtain

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\,2^{(\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)/2}-1=\big(2^{\Delta/2}-1\big)+2^{\Delta/2}\Big(\log\!\Big(\frac{L}{(L!)^{1/L}}\Big)+\frac{\log 2}{2}\Big)\beta^{\prime}+o(\beta^{\prime}).
``` |  |

Therefore, as $`\beta^{\prime}\to 0`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\nu^{\ell}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})=\frac{c(1-\gamma)^{3/2}\log\!\Big(\frac{L}{(L!)^{1/L}}\Big)}{2c_{\delta}\big(2^{\Delta/2}-1\big)}\,\beta^{\prime}\;+\;o(\beta^{\prime}),
``` |  |

which is consistent with
Lemma [C.7](#A3.Thmtheorem7 "Lemma C.7. ‣ C.4 Proof of Corollary ‣ C. Proofs for Section ‣ Appendix: Proofs ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula").
Therefore, when $`\beta^{\prime}`$ is sufficiently small, the above
expansion implies that $`\nu^{\ell}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})`$ is
strictly increasing in $`\beta^{\prime}`$.

We further characterize the shape of this auxiliary root. Direct
differentiation shows that
$`\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\beta^{\prime}}\nu^{\ell}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})=0`$
is equivalent to

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})-1\big)}{a_{L}^{\prime}(\beta^{\prime})}=\frac{2^{(\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)/2}-1}{\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\beta^{\prime}}2^{(\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)/2}}.
``` |  |

Let the right-hand side be denoted by $`g(\beta^{\prime})`$. Then

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
g(\beta^{\prime}):=\frac{2^{(\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)/2}-1}{\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\beta^{\prime}}2^{(\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)/2}}=\frac{2}{\log 2}\left(1-2^{-(\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)/2}\right).
``` |  |

This is strictly increasing in $`\beta^{\prime}`$, and satisfies

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
g(0)=\frac{2}{\log 2}\left(1-2^{-\Delta/2}\right)>0,\qquad\lim_{\beta^{\prime}\to\infty}g(\beta^{\prime})=\frac{2}{\log 2}.
``` |  |

Let the left-hand side be denoted by $`h(\beta^{\prime})`$, namely

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
h(\beta^{\prime}):=\frac{a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})-1\big)}{a_{L}^{\prime}(\beta^{\prime})}.
``` |  |

Lemma [C.9](#A3.Thmtheorem9 "Lemma C.9. ‣ C.4 Proof of Corollary ‣ C. Proofs for Section ‣ Appendix: Proofs ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula")
shows that $`h(\beta^{\prime})`$ is also strictly increasing in
$`\beta^{\prime}`$. Moreover,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\lim_{\beta^{\prime}\to 0}h(\beta^{\prime})=0,\qquad\lim_{\beta^{\prime}\to\infty}h(\beta^{\prime})=+\infty.
``` |  |

Thus $`h^{-1}`$ is well-defined on $`(0,\infty)`$. By
Lemma [C.10](#A3.Thmtheorem10 "Lemma C.10. ‣ C.4 Proof of Corollary ‣ C. Proofs for Section ‣ Appendix: Proofs ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula"),
$`h`$ is strictly convex in $`\beta^{\prime}`$, while
$`g^{\prime\prime}(\beta^{\prime})=-\frac{\log 2}{2}2^{-(\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)/2}<0.`$
Hence, $`h(\beta^{\prime})-g(\beta^{\prime})`$ is strictly convex. Since

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
h(0)-g(0)<0,\qquad\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\beta^{\prime}}\big(h(\beta^{\prime})-g(\beta^{\prime})\big)\Big|_{\beta^{\prime}=0}=1-2^{-\Delta/2}>0,
``` |  |

and
$`\lim_{\beta^{\prime}\to\infty}(h(\beta^{\prime})-g(\beta^{\prime}))=+\infty`$,
the equation $`h(\beta^{\prime})=g(\beta^{\prime})`$ admits a unique
solution, denoted by $`\bar{\beta}^{\prime}_{s}`$. Equivalently,
$`\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\beta^{\prime}}\nu^{\ell}_{\Delta}(\bar{\beta}^{\prime}_{s})=0.`$
Furthermore, since
$`h(\bar{\beta}^{\prime}_{s})=g(\bar{\beta}^{\prime}_{s})`$ and
$`g(\bar{\beta}^{\prime}_{s})=\frac{2}{\log 2}\bigl(1-2^{-(\bar{\beta}^{\prime}_{s}+\Delta)/2}\bigr)<\frac{2}{\log 2}`$,
the strict monotonicity of $`h`$ gives
$`\bar{\beta}^{\prime}_{s}<h^{-1}\!\left(\frac{2}{\log 2}\right)`$.

Choose $`B>h^{-1}\!\left(\frac{2}{\log 2}\right)`$, we have
$`\bar{\beta}^{\prime}_{s}\in(0,B).`$ Thus
$`\nu^{\ell}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})`$ is first increasing and then
decreasing on $`[0,B]`$, with a unique optimizer on this interval. We
now show that the true threshold
$`\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})`$ inherits the same monotonicity
pattern on $`[0,B]`$ when $`\nu_{T}`$ is sufficiently small.

On the one hand, since

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\partial_{\nu}\mathcal{N}(\beta^{\prime},\nu)=\frac{c_{\delta}}{c\sqrt{1-\gamma}}\Big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\,2^{(\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)/2}-1\Big)+\partial_{\nu}R_{B}(\beta^{\prime},\nu),
``` |  |

$`\big|\partial_{\nu}R_{B}(\beta^{\prime},\nu)\big|\leq C_{1}(B)\nu\leq C_{1}(B)\nu_{T},`$
and
$`\frac{c_{\delta}}{c\sqrt{1-\gamma}}\Big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\,2^{(\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)/2}-1\Big)`$
admits a strictly positive lower bound on $`[0,B]`$, it follows that

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\partial_{\nu}\mathcal{N}(\beta^{\prime},\nu)=\frac{c_{\delta}}{c\sqrt{1-\gamma}}\Big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\,2^{(\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)/2}-1\Big)\bigl(1+O_{B}(\nu_{T})\bigr).
``` |  |

On the other hand,
$`\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}\mathcal{N}(\beta^{\prime},\nu)=\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}\mathcal{N}_{\ell}(\beta^{\prime},\nu)+\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}R_{B}(\beta^{\prime},\nu),`$
so plugging in $`\nu=\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})`$ gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}\mathcal{N}\big(\beta^{\prime},\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})\big)=\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}\mathcal{N}_{\ell}\big(\beta^{\prime},\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})\big)+\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}R_{B}\big(\beta^{\prime},\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})\big).
``` |  |

Moreover, since

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}\mathcal{N}_{\ell}(\beta^{\prime},\nu)=-\frac{1}{2}a_{L}^{\prime}(\beta^{\prime})(1-\gamma)+\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\beta^{\prime}}\!\left[\frac{c_{\delta}}{c\sqrt{1-\gamma}}\Big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\,2^{(\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)/2}-1\Big)\right]\nu,
``` |  |

we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}\mathcal{N}_{\ell}\big(\beta^{\prime},\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})\big)=\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}\mathcal{N}_{\ell}\big(\beta^{\prime},\nu^{\ell}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})\big)+\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\beta^{\prime}}\!\left[\frac{c_{\delta}}{c\sqrt{1-\gamma}}\Big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\,2^{(\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)/2}-1\Big)\right]\Big(\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})-\nu^{\ell}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})\Big).`$ |  |

Hence

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}\mathcal{N}\big(\beta^{\prime},\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})\big)`$ | $`\displaystyle=\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}\mathcal{N}_{\ell}\big(\beta^{\prime},\nu^{\ell}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})\big)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\beta^{\prime}}\!\left[\frac{c_{\delta}}{c\sqrt{1-\gamma}}\Big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\,2^{(\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)/2}-1\Big)\right]\Big(\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})-\nu^{\ell}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})\Big)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}R_{B}\big(\beta^{\prime},\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})\big).`$ |  |

For the remainder derivative, we use the bound
$`\big|\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}R_{B}(\beta^{\prime},\nu)\big|\leq C_{2}(B)\nu^{2}\leq C_{2}(B)\nu_{T}^{2}.`$
Next, to control
$`\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})-\nu^{\ell}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})`$,
note that $`\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})`$ satisfies

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
-\frac{1}{2}\big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})-1\big)(1-\gamma)+\frac{c_{\delta}}{c\sqrt{1-\gamma}}\Big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\,2^{(\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)/2}-1\Big)\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})+R_{B}\big(\beta^{\prime},\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})\big)=0,
``` |  |

while $`\nu^{\ell}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})`$ satisfies

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
-\frac{1}{2}\big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})-1\big)(1-\gamma)+\frac{c_{\delta}}{c\sqrt{1-\gamma}}\Big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\,2^{(\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)/2}-1\Big)\nu^{\ell}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})=0.
``` |  |

Subtracting the two equations yields

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})-\nu^{\ell}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})=-\frac{R_{B}\big(\beta^{\prime},\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})\big)}{\frac{c_{\delta}}{c\sqrt{1-\gamma}}\Big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\,2^{(\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)/2}-1\Big)}.
``` |  |

Since
$`|R_{B}(\beta^{\prime},\nu)|\leq C_{0}(B)\nu^{2}\leq C_{0}(B)\nu_{T}^{2},`$
and the denominator admits a strictly positive lower bound on $`[0,B]`$,
we conclude that
$`\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})-\nu^{\ell}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})=O_{B}(\nu_{T}^{2}).`$
Therefore, because the derivative
$`\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\beta^{\prime}}\!\left[\frac{c_{\delta}}{c\sqrt{1-\gamma}}\Big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\,2^{(\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)/2}-1\Big)\right]`$
is bounded on $`[0,B]`$, we obtain

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}\mathcal{N}\big(\beta^{\prime},\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})\big)=\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}\mathcal{N}_{\ell}\big(\beta^{\prime},\nu^{\ell}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})\big)+O_{B}(\nu_{T}^{2}),
``` |  |

uniformly for $`\beta^{\prime}\in[0,B]`$.

Then, by the implicit function theorem,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\beta^{\prime}}\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})=-\frac{\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}\mathcal{N}\big(\beta^{\prime},\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})\big)}{\partial_{\nu}\mathcal{N}\big(\beta^{\prime},\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})\big)}=-\frac{\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}\mathcal{N}_{\ell}\big(\beta^{\prime},\nu^{\ell}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})\big)+O_{B}(\nu_{T}^{2})}{\frac{c_{\delta}}{c\sqrt{1-\gamma}}\Big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\,2^{(\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)/2}-1\Big)\bigl(1+O_{B}(\nu_{T})\bigr)}.
``` |  |

Equivalently,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\beta^{\prime}}\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})=-\frac{\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}\mathcal{N}_{\ell}\big(\beta^{\prime},\nu^{\ell}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})\big)}{\frac{c_{\delta}}{c\sqrt{1-\gamma}}\Big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\,2^{(\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)/2}-1\Big)}+O_{B}(\nu_{T}).
``` |  |

Therefore,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\beta^{\prime}}\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\beta^{\prime}}\nu^{\ell}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})+O_{B}(\nu_{T}),
``` |  |

uniformly on $`[0,B]`$. Since $`\nu^{\ell}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})`$ is
first increasing and then decreasing on $`[0,B]`$, with a unique
optimizer on this interval, the above uniform perturbation implies that,
by taking $`\nu_{0}(B)>0`$ sufficiently small, whenever
$`\nu_{T}<\nu_{0}(B)`$, the map
$`\beta^{\prime}\mapsto\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})`$ is also
first increasing and then decreasing on $`[0,B]`$, with a unique
optimizer on this interval. This proves the first part of the lemma.

It remains to prove the large-$`\beta^{\prime}`$ behavior. By the
standing well-definedness requirement of
$`\mathcal{N}(\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}+\Delta,\nu,x_{0})`$, every
radicand appearing in the denominators must remain strictly positive. In
particular, since $`\beta=\beta^{\prime}+\Delta`$, we must have
$`2^{-(\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)}(1-\gamma)-c_{\delta^{\prime}}\,\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})>0.`$
Therefore,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})<\frac{1-\gamma}{c_{\delta^{\prime}}}2^{-(\beta^{\prime}+\Delta)}=O(2^{-\beta^{\prime}}).
``` |  |

It follows immediately that
$`\nu^{\star}_{\Delta}(\beta^{\prime})\to 0`$ as
$`\beta^{\prime}\to\infty.`$ This completes the proof. ∎

###### Lemma C.9.

Let $`L\geq 2`$ be an integer and define
$`a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\coloneqq\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{L}\Big(\frac{L}{i}\Big)^{\beta^{\prime}}.`$
Then the mapping

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\beta^{\prime}\ \longmapsto\ \frac{a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})-1\big)}{a_{L}^{\prime}(\beta^{\prime})},\qquad\beta^{\prime}>0,
``` |  |

is strictly increasing on $`(0,\infty)`$. Moreover, it satisfies

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\lim_{\beta^{\prime}\to 0}\frac{a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})-1\big)}{a_{L}^{\prime}(\beta^{\prime})}=0,\qquad\lim_{\beta^{\prime}\to\infty}\frac{a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})-1\big)}{a_{L}^{\prime}(\beta^{\prime})}=+\infty.
``` |  |

###### Proof.

Define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
g(\beta^{\prime})\coloneqq\frac{a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})-1\big)}{a_{L}^{\prime}(\beta^{\prime})}.
``` |  |

We first prove that $`g(\beta^{\prime})`$ is increasing on
$`(0,\infty)`$. For any $`i\in[L]`$, define
$`x_{i}\coloneqq\log\!\Big(\frac{L}{i}\Big).`$ Then
$`x_{i}\in[0,\log L]`$, and we can rewrite

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})=\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{L}e^{\beta^{\prime}x_{i}}.
``` |  |

We define a random variable $`X`$ supported on
$`\{x_{1},x_{2},\dots,x_{L}\}`$ with probability mass function

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
p_{i}\coloneqq\frac{e^{\beta^{\prime}x_{i}}}{\sum_{j=1}^{L}e^{\beta^{\prime}x_{j}}}=\frac{e^{\beta^{\prime}x_{i}}}{L\,a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})},\qquad i=1,\dots,L.
``` |  |

Note that this notation $`p_{i}`$ is unrelated to the “question
distribution” used elsewhere in the paper; we reuse the symbol $`p`$
here only to follow standard convention. With this definition, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
a_{L}^{\prime}(\beta^{\prime})=\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{L}x_{i}e^{\beta^{\prime}x_{i}}=a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\,\mathbb{E}[X],\qquad a_{L}^{\prime\prime}(\beta^{\prime})=\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{L}x_{i}^{2}e^{\beta^{\prime}x_{i}}=a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\,\mathbb{E}[X^{2}].
``` |  |

Moreover, note that

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\beta^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}[X]=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\beta^{\prime}}\Big(\frac{a_{L}^{\prime}(\beta^{\prime})}{a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})}\Big)=\frac{a_{L}^{\prime\prime}(\beta^{\prime})a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})-\big(a_{L}^{\prime}(\beta^{\prime})\big)^{2}}{\big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\big)^{2}}=\mathbb{E}[X^{2}]-\mathbb{E}[X]^{2}.
``` |  |

Therefore,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
g(\beta^{\prime})=\frac{a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})-1\big)}{a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\,\mathbb{E}[X]}=\frac{a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})-1}{\mathbb{E}[X]}.
``` |  |

Differentiating yields

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
g^{\prime}(\beta^{\prime})=\frac{a_{L}^{\prime}(\beta^{\prime})\,\mathbb{E}[X]-\big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})-1\big)\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\beta^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}[X]}{\mathbb{E}[X]^{2}}=\frac{a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\,\mathbb{E}[X]^{2}-\big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})-1\big)\Var(X)}{\mathbb{E}[X]^{2}}.
``` |  |

Hence, to prove that $`g(\beta^{\prime})`$ is increasing, it suffices to
show that $`g^{\prime}(\beta^{\prime})>0`$, i.e.,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Var(X)<\frac{a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})}{a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})-1}\,\mathbb{E}[X]^{2}.
``` |  |

By
Lemma [C.11](#A3.Thmtheorem11 "Lemma C.11. ‣ C.4 Proof of Corollary ‣ C. Proofs for Section ‣ Appendix: Proofs ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula")
with $`k=L`$, the distribution of $`X_{L}`$ is the same as the
distribution of $`X`$. Indeed, since
$`e^{\beta^{\prime}x_{i}}=\left(\frac{L}{i}\right)^{\beta^{\prime}}=L^{\beta^{\prime}}i^{-\beta^{\prime}},`$
the normalizing factor $`L^{\beta^{\prime}}`$ cancels, and hence
$`p_{i}=\frac{e^{\beta^{\prime}x_{i}}}{\sum_{j=1}^{L}e^{\beta^{\prime}x_{j}}}=\frac{i^{-\beta^{\prime}}}{\sum_{j=1}^{L}j^{-\beta^{\prime}}}.`$
Therefore,
Lemma [C.11](#A3.Thmtheorem11 "Lemma C.11. ‣ C.4 Proof of Corollary ‣ C. Proofs for Section ‣ Appendix: Proofs ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula")
gives $`\mathbb{E}[X^{2}]<2\mathbb{E}[X]^{2}.`$ It follows that

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Var(X)=\mathbb{E}[X^{2}]-\mathbb{E}[X]^{2}<\mathbb{E}[X]^{2}<\frac{a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})}{a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})-1}\,\mathbb{E}[X]^{2}.
``` |  |

It remains to verify the two limits. By a first-order Taylor expansion,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})=\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{L}e^{\beta^{\prime}x_{i}}=1+\beta^{\prime}\cdot\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{L}x_{i}+o(\beta^{\prime}),\qquad a_{L}^{\prime}(\beta^{\prime})=\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{L}x_{i}e^{\beta^{\prime}x_{i}}=\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{L}x_{i}+o(1),
``` |  |

as $`\beta^{\prime}\to 0`$. Since $`\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{L}x_{i}>0`$,
it follows that

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\lim_{\beta^{\prime}\to 0}g(\beta^{\prime})=\lim_{\beta^{\prime}\to 0}\frac{a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})-1\big)}{a_{L}^{\prime}(\beta^{\prime})}=\lim_{\beta^{\prime}\to 0}\frac{\big(1+o(1)\big)\big(\beta^{\prime}\cdot\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{L}x_{i}+o(\beta^{\prime})\big)}{\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{L}x_{i}+o(1)}=0.
``` |  |

As $`\beta^{\prime}\to\infty`$, the sum defining
$`a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})`$ is dominated by its largest term, i.e.,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})=\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{L}e^{\beta^{\prime}x_{i}}\sim\frac{1}{L}e^{\beta^{\prime}x_{1}},\qquad a_{L}^{\prime}(\beta^{\prime})=\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{L}x_{i}e^{\beta^{\prime}x_{i}}\sim\frac{1}{L}x_{1}e^{\beta^{\prime}x_{1}},
``` |  |

and hence

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}[X]=\frac{a_{L}^{\prime}(\beta^{\prime})}{a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})}\to x_{1}=\log L,\qquad a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\to\infty.
``` |  |

Therefore,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\lim_{\beta^{\prime}\to\infty}g(\beta^{\prime})=\lim_{\beta^{\prime}\to\infty}\frac{a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})-1}{\mathbb{E}[X]}=+\infty.
``` |  |

This completes the proof. ∎

###### Lemma C.10.

Let $`L\geq 2`$ be an integer and define
$`a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\coloneqq\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{L}\Big(\frac{L}{i}\Big)^{\beta^{\prime}}.`$
Then the mapping

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\beta^{\prime}\ \longmapsto\ \frac{a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})-1\big)}{a_{L}^{\prime}(\beta^{\prime})},\qquad\beta^{\prime}>0,
``` |  |

is strictly convex on $`(0,\infty)`$.

###### Proof.

As in
Lemma [C.9](#A3.Thmtheorem9 "Lemma C.9. ‣ C.4 Proof of Corollary ‣ C. Proofs for Section ‣ Appendix: Proofs ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula"),
define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
g(\beta^{\prime})\coloneqq\frac{a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})-1\big)}{a_{L}^{\prime}(\beta^{\prime})}.
``` |  |

For $`i\in[L]`$, define
$`x_{i}\coloneqq\log\!\left(\frac{L}{i}\right).`$ Let $`X`$ be the
random variable supported on $`\{x_{1},\dots,x_{L}\}`$ with probability
mass function

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
p_{i}\coloneqq\frac{e^{\beta^{\prime}x_{i}}}{\sum_{j=1}^{L}e^{\beta^{\prime}x_{j}}}=\frac{e^{\beta^{\prime}x_{i}}}{L\,a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})},\qquad i=1,\dots,L.
``` |  |

Then
$`a_{L}^{\prime}(\beta^{\prime})=a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\mathbb{E}[X]`$
and $`g(\beta^{\prime})=\frac{a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})-1}{\mathbb{E}[X]}.`$
We also have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\beta^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}[X]=\mathbb{E}[X^{2}]-\mathbb{E}[X]^{2},
``` |  |

and

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\beta^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}[X^{2}]=\mathbb{E}[X^{3}]-\mathbb{E}[X]\mathbb{E}[X^{2}].
``` |  |

Differentiating $`g(\beta^{\prime})`$ gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle g^{\prime}(\beta^{\prime})=\frac{a_{L}^{\prime}(\beta^{\prime})\mathbb{E}[X]-\big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})-1\big)\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\beta^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}[X]}{\mathbb{E}[X]^{2}}=1+\big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})-1\big)\left(2-\frac{\mathbb{E}[X^{2}]}{\mathbb{E}[X]^{2}}\right).`$ |  |

Hence

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle g^{\prime\prime}(\beta^{\prime})`$ | $`\displaystyle=a_{L}^{\prime}(\beta^{\prime})\left(2-\frac{\mathbb{E}[X^{2}]}{\mathbb{E}[X]^{2}}\right)-\big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})-1\big)\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\beta^{\prime}}\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}[X^{2}]}{\mathbb{E}[X]^{2}}\right)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})\mathbb{E}[X]\left(2-\frac{\mathbb{E}[X^{2}]}{\mathbb{E}[X]^{2}}\right)-\big(a_{L}(\beta^{\prime})-1\big)\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\beta^{\prime}}\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}[X^{2}]}{\mathbb{E}[X]^{2}}\right).`$ |  |

Moreover,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\beta^{\prime}}\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}[X^{2}]}{\mathbb{E}[X]^{2}}\right)`$ | $`\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}[X]\mathbb{E}[X^{3}]+\mathbb{E}[X]^{2}\mathbb{E}[X^{2}]-2\mathbb{E}[X^{2}]^{2}}{\mathbb{E}[X]^{3}}.`$ |  |

Now apply
Lemma [C.11](#A3.Thmtheorem11 "Lemma C.11. ‣ C.4 Proof of Corollary ‣ C. Proofs for Section ‣ Appendix: Proofs ‣ A Task-Centric Theory for Iterative Self-Improvement with Easy-to-Hard Curricula")
````
